Both scopes exhausted, native allocation complete; no all-simplex/W conclusion.", "evidence": "# Radial Gray vertices: an original-pair obstruction and a paid inverse-law screen\n\n**JOINT UNVERIFIED RESULT.** Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`.\nOne bounded direct analytical allocation; exactly TWO substantive laws.\nAll new assertions below form one joint proof draft. No assertion in this\nfile has been independently verified or entered into the fact graph.\n\nThe first law is impossible on the original physical word: its first two\nroot-saturated transitions force a strictly positive ORIGINAL remote\nprojection-normal row at query 2. This holds for every declared parameter,\nincluding every later bridge parameter theta and every vertical scale R.\nThe second law uses an explicit unequal-radius dictionary and the finite\npower-score inverse rule. Its complete actual trajectory, all original\nrows, full minimum cone, and exact closest-minimum price are derived.\nIts supremum is exactly the complete-word Huber score 1/D_m, and terminals\non the segment within one vertex length of the chosen closest minimum\nhave a vanishing relative score. Neither result excludes all radial\nsimplices, all inverse terminals, or all smooth convex functions.\n\n## 1. Original target and the exact scope of this allocation\n\nFor every finite dimension, every convex differentiable f with\nL-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, every minimizer x_star,\nand every initial point different from that minimizer, the original\nnormalization is\n\n    W_n(h) = sup 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2),\n    x_(i+1) = x_i - (h_i/L) grad f(x_i),\n    W_n^star = inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h),  W = 2 R.\n\nThe original goal is W_n^star/U_n^ZJ -> 1, with\nU_n^ZJ = 1/(2 S_bullet(n)+1) = 1/B_n. Its affirmative quantifiers are:\nfor every eta>0, one threshold N_eta works for EVERY n>=N_eta and EVERY\npredetermined nonnegative word of length n. A negative answer requires a\nfull-function worst-case upper certificate giving a nonvanishing\nseparation along an unbounded subsequence. Neither an obstruction to a\nselected witness nor an attained lower-witness score is that certificate.\nThe complete `PROBLEM.md` is unchanged; its benchmark uses all integer\nsplits and is not replaced by a dyadic or exponent-only surrogate.\n\nHere, throughout every child and every formula, fix the SAME integer\nm>=1536 and put\n\n    N=2^m, n=N-1, epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_l=1+a c^l, 0<=l<m,\n    h_i=H_(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n,\n    S=sum_(i=0)^(n-1) h_i, D_m=1+2S.\n\nThus H_0=1+a, H_1=1+beta, and all H_l with l>=2 exceed 2.\nFor later elementary signs it suffices to note\n\n    0<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2, c>2, S>n>=1.\n\nIndeed epsilon<=1/6144<1/100, beta>99/100,\nc<(49/20)(101/100)<5/2, and a>99/250>1/3;\na<1/(1+sqrt(2))<1/2. Also beta c> (99/100)*2>1,\nwhich proves H_2>2 and hence the assertion about all higher levels.\nThese are symbolic inequalities, not evaluated numerical trajectories.\n\nThe complete cut functional referenced in the public task is\n\n    K_n(h)=max { 1/(1+2 sum h),\n       max_(0<=i<n) (h_i-1)_+^2 /\n       [(1+sum_(t<i)h_t)^2 (1+2sum_(t>i)h_t)] }.\n\nOur comparison with the COMPLETE K_n uses only its first entry:\nK_n(h)>=1/(1+2 sum h). We do not choose a favorable single cut and call\nit the whole channel. The task also supplies the accepted scoped input\n931b9653ce0f7201, B_n/D_m<47/50 on this hierarchy. This input is used\nonly to multiply an UPPER bound on this selected family's attained\nscore. It is never reversed into a lower-witness gain. The exact\n1/D_m conclusions below do not depend on that input.\n\n## 2. One body, all coordinates, and the full minimum set\n\nLet G=F_2^m. For every nonzero character label t and every s in G set\n\n    chi_t(s)=(-1)^(t dot s),\n    U_s=(b_t chi_t(s))_(t!=0) in R^(N-1),\n    Kmix=N-1-m,\n    b_(e_l)^2=2^l/(N-1), 0<=l<m,\n    b_t^2=zeta/(N^2 Kmix) otherwise, 0<zeta<=1.\n\nAll N-1 coordinates remain present and strictly positive. Introduce\npositive R and define\n\n    M=sum_(t!=0)b_t^2=1+zeta/N^2,\n    T=M+1/R^2,\n    w_s=(U_s,1/R) in R^N.\n\nFor positive radii sigma_i and the physical Gray labels Gamma(i), put\n\n    v_i=sigma_i w_(Gamma(i)), 0<=i<=n,\n    D=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}, C=cone{v_0,...,v_n}.\n\nThe physical Euclidean inner product is unchanged. In particular the\nvertical coordinate contributes sigma_i sigma_j/R^2 to every Gram\nentry and sigma_i^2/R^2 to every vertex norm. It is not a free carrier.\n\nPairing labels that differ in a bit gives\n\n    sum_s chi_t(s)=0 for t!=0,\n    sum_s chi_t(s)chi_u(s)=N*1_(t=u),\n\nwhere the same statement includes the constant character when needed.\nConsequently the full Walsh matrix is invertible. Since all b_t and\n1/R are nonzero, the N vectors w_s are linearly independent. Multiplying\nthe columns by positive radii preserves this property. Thus D is one\nfull-dimensional simplex in R^N with N+1=n+2 vertices, including 0.\nThis is proved directly; the separate unverified simplex-universality\ncandidate is not a premise.\n\nLet\n\n    K(s,u)=<U_s,U_u>,\n    E(d)=sum_(t:t dot d=1)b_t^2.\n\nThen\n\n    K(s,u)=M-2E(s+u),\n    <w_s,w_u>=T-2E(s+u), ||w_s||^2=T.\n\nThe cone C is independent of the positive radii. It is closed: the\nlinear map with the independent columns w_s is invertible and maps the\nclosed nonnegative orthant onto C. Its full polar cone is\n\n    C^polar = { (x,y): <x,U_s>+y/R<=0 for EVERY s in G }\n            = { (x,y): y<=-R max_s <x,U_s> }.\n\nDefine the single static objective\n\n    F_D(z)=max_(g in D) { <z,g>-||g||^2/2 }.\n\nCompleting the square proves that its maximizer is the unique Euclidean\nprojection P_D(z). For g in D the exact condition is\n\n    <z-g,w-g><=0 for every original vertex w of D, including 0.  (2.1)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating squared distance along each\nsegment from g; sufficiency follows by expansion and convex combination.\nSumming this condition at two points gives\n\n    <z-z',P_D(z)-P_D(z')> >= ||P_D(z)-P_D(z')||^2.\n\nIt follows that P_D is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing affine\nexpressions shows that the first-order remainder of F_D lies between\n0 and ||z-z'||^2, so F_D is differentiable with gradient P_D. It is\nfinite and convex. An open subset of D has projection equal to the\nidentity, so its least global Lipschitz gradient constant is exactly 1.\n\nBecause 0 belongs to D, F_D>=0. If z belongs to C^polar, every inner\nproduct <z,g> is nonpositive and F_D(z)=0. Conversely, if <z,v_j>>0,\nthe segment t v_j in D gives\n\n    F_D(z)>=t<z,v_j> - t^2||v_j||^2/2 >0\n\nfor a sufficiently small positive t. Hence the FULL attained minimum is\n\n    argmin F_D=C^polar, min F_D=0.                         (2.2)\n\nIn particular z_star=(0,-R) is a strict polar point since\n<z_star,v_i>=-sigma_i. For an initialization z_0=(x_0,-R), the distance\nto this particular minimizer is ||x_0||, but it need not be the distance\nto the whole minimum cone. Section 6 computes the latter exactly for\nthe legal law. Section 4 proves that the first proposed law has no\ninitialization at all, even without the slice y=-R.\n\nMultiplying F_D by any L>0 gives an L-smooth objective with the same\nphysical iterates under step h_i/L and the same W=2R score.\n\n## 3. Physical Gray order and the adjacent necessary condition\n\nLet Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2), with m binary bits. If l=nu_2(i+1),\nthen the bits of i below l are 1 and bit l is 0. Incrementing i flips\nexactly bits 0 through l. Taking xor with the shifted label cancels\nthese changes except at bit l. Thus\n\n    Gamma(i+1)=Gamma(i)+e_l, l=nu_2(i+1).                  (3.1)\n\nThis is the original chronological order, not a permutation of the\nword. In particular the first three labels are\n\n    Gamma(0)=0, Gamma(1)=e_0, Gamma(2)=e_0+e_1,\n\nand the two corresponding physical steps are H_0 and H_1.\n\nFor a fixed bit l, exactly N/2 character labels have t_l=1. Of these,\nexactly one is independent, namely e_l. Consequently\n\n    E_l=E(e_l)=2^l/(N-1)+zeta (N/2-1)/(N^2 Kmix),\n    t_l=1-2E_l/T.                                        (3.2)\n\nEvery E_l is strictly between 0 and M, so -1<t_l<1.\nFor an adjacent pair p=sigma w_s and q=sigma r w_(s+e_l),\nsuppose one physical step H=H_l gives\n\n    P_D(z)=p, z'=z-Hp, P_D(z')=q.\n\nWrite d=q-p, r_z=z-p, r_z'=z'-q. The two original normal\ninequalities against the other vertex are\n\n    <r_z,d><=0, <r_z',d>>=0,\n    r_z-r_z'=(H-1)p+q.\n\nTherefore\n\n    <(H-1)p+q,q-p><=0,\n\nor, after dividing by sigma^2 T,\n\n    r^2+(H-2)t_l r+1-H<=0.                              (3.3)\n\nThe constant term is negative because H>1, so the quadratic has one\nnegative root and one positive root. The positive root is exactly\n\n    rho_l=(-(H_l-2)t_l+\n        sqrt((H_l-2)^2 t_l^2+4(H_l-1)))/2.\n\nAt r=1 its polynomial equals (2-H_l)(1-t_l). Thus\n\n    0<rho_l<1 if H_l<2,\n    rho_l>1 if H_l>2.\n\nWhen r=rho_l, (3.3) is equality. The two original normal\ninequalities then have the SAME inner product, one nonpositive and one\nnonnegative, and hence\n\n    <r_z,d>=<r_z',d>=0.                                 (3.4)\n\nThis equality consequence is the source of the remote obstruction.\nCondition (3.3) by itself is only necessary, not sufficient for a\nsingle static projection potential or for global interpolation.\n\n## 4. Law I: uniform rejection by an original remote row\n\nThe first law is exactly\n\n    sigma_0=1,\n    sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i r_(nu_2(i+1)),\n    r_l=rho_l for H_l<2,\n    r_l=1+theta(rho_l-1) for H_l>2, 0<=theta<=1.           (4.1)\n\n**Claim I, joint draft.** For EVERY m>=1536, R>0,\n0<zeta<=1, theta in [0,1], there is NO point z_0 in R^N for which\nP_D(z_i)=v_i at all the prescribed Gray queries under the original GD\nsteps. Already queries 0,1,2 are inconsistent. In particular no explicit\nx_0 in the required slice z_0=(x_0,-R) can exist. Every later radius or\nbridge choice, even one different from (4.1), is irrelevant to this\nobstruction if these first two root ratios are retained.\n\nProof. Abbreviate\n\n    u=rho_0, v=rho_1, e=E_0, f=E_1,\n    J=sum_(t:t_0=t_1=1)b_t^2=zeta/(4N Kmix).\n\nThe formula for J uses the N/4 labels with both bits 1; none is an\nindependent label. The first three gradient vectors would be\n\n    p_0=w_0, p_1=u w_(e_0), p_2=u v w_(e_0+e_1).\n\nThey have the exact inner products\n\n    <w_0,w_(e_0)>=T-2e,\n    <w_(e_0),w_(e_0+e_1)>=T-2f,\n    <w_0,w_(e_0+e_1)>=T-2e-2f+4J.                      (4.2)\n\nThe last identity is the inclusion-exclusion formula for characters\nthat change sign in exactly one of bits 0 and 1. The vertical part has\nalready been included in T in every one of these entries.\n\nLet z_1=z_0-(1+a)p_0, z_2=z_1-(1+beta)p_1, and\nlet r_i=z_i-p_i. If the three projections existed, (3.4), once for\neach of the root-saturated adjacent pairs, would give\n\n    <r_1,p_1-p_0>=0, <r_1,p_2-p_1>=0.\n\nThus <r_1,p_2-p_0>=0. Also\n\n    r_2=r_1-beta p_1-p_2.\n\nThe ORIGINAL query-2 normal against the ORIGINAL vertex p_0 requires\n\n    <r_2,p_0-p_2><=0.\n\nBut the left side is forced to equal\n\n    B=(beta p_1+p_2) dot (p_2-p_0)\n     =(beta p_1+p_2) dot (p_1-p_0),                     (4.3)\n\nwhere the last equality uses the root equality at the second physical\nstep. Substituting (4.2) and collecting terms gives exactly\n\n    B=u[(beta+v) A + 2 v f(1-u) - 4 v J],\n    A=T(u-1)+2e.                                       (4.4)\n\nWe now prove B>0 uniformly, without an approximation in 1/N or 1/R.\nThe level-0 root equation is\n\n    u^2-(1-a)(1-2e/T)u-a=0.\n\nRearranging it gives\n\n    (1-u)(u+a)=2(1-a)e u/T,\n    A=2e a(1+u)/(u+a)>2e a,                            (4.5)\n\nsince a<1 and u>0. Both u and v lie strictly between 0 and 1 by\nSection 3. Thus the middle term in (4.4) is strictly positive and\n\n    B > u v (2e a-4J).\n\nIt remains to check this last sign over the entire domain. For m>=3,\nN=2^m>=8 and Kmix=N-1-m>=4; the latter follows at m=3 and increases\nby 2^m-1 when m is incremented. Also e>=1/(N-1), so\n\n    4J/e <= (N-1)/(N Kmix) < 1/Kmix <= 1/4,\n    2a>2/3>1/4.\n\nTherefore 2e a>4J and B>0. This contradicts the required normal row.\nIt proves Claim I for every m in the requested domain, with no\nrestriction on R and with no dependence on theta. QED.\n\nThis is not a finite-case scan or a sign guessed from small m. It is\none symbolic contradiction uniform in all declared parameters. It is\nvalue-independent within the declared static-body construction: x_0\ncancels, and F_D values have no free offsets that could repair the\nnormal inequality. It is NOT asserted as an impossibility for arbitrary\nsmooth interpolation with unrelated conjugate offsets, or for every\nradial dictionary. In particular a different character-intersection\nstructure changes J in (4.4) and is outside the rejected scope.\n\n## 5. Every original interpolation row and the inverse rule\n\nThese identities are derived jointly and will justify the second law.\nFor any actual projection run of this same F_D, set\n\n    g_i=P_D(z_i), r_i=z_i-g_i,\n    f_i=<z_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,\n    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i g_i.\n\nFor every ordered pair of original indices, including terminal\ndirections, direct expansion gives\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                              (5.1)\n\nFor any minimizer q in C^polar, give it gradient 0 and value 0. Then\n\n    Q_(i,star)=<g_i,q><=0,\n    Q_(star,i)=-<r_i,g_i><=0,                           (5.2)\n\nthe second inequality following from the normal test against vertex 0.\nThus all ordered original rows, all minimizer rows, and the real PSD\nGram follow from the body rather than a selected subset of comparisons.\n\nFor completeness the finite inverse rule also has a direct proof.\nFor a finite set of dictionary vertices V including 0, fix h>1 and y,\nchoose a vertex v maximizing\n\n    score_y,h(v)=<y,v>+(h-1)||v||^2/2,\n\nand put x=y+h v. For every original dictionary vertex w,\n\n    <x-v,w-v>\n      =score_y,h(w)-score_y,h(v)-(h-1)||w-v||^2/2<=0.\n\nCondition (2.1) therefore proves P_D(x)=v, and x-hP_D(x)=y.\nThis proof includes the zero vertex and every remote vertex. It is\nonly a legality rule until an initial-radius/terminal bound is added.\n\n## 6. Law II: an explicit inverse terminal with an exact full price\n\nUse the SAME dictionary construction with theta=0 in (4.1); do not\nprescribe its Gray order as the itinerary. Thus r_0=rho_0<1,\nr_1=rho_1<1, and r_l=1 for l>=2. Keep all the N+1 original vertices.\nFor i>=1, sigma_i<sigma_0=1, because all ratios are at most 1 and the\nfirst ratio is strictly less than 1. Consequently the explicit vertex\n\n    p=v_0=w_0=(U_0,1/R)\n\nis the UNIQUE maximum-norm vertex; ||p||^2=T. This is a genuinely\nunequal-radius dictionary, although its actual selected itinerary will\nbe constant. No maximum search or feasibility optimization is required\nto specify p.\n\nFor an explicit scalar t>=0, let A_0=S+t and set\n\n    lambda_t=(1+A_0/R^2)/M,\n    q_t=(lambda_t U_0,-lambda_t M R),\n    y_t=q_t+t p. Hence q_t belongs to the complete minimum cone (2.2).\nThe terminal is within t||p|| of this explicitly chosen polar minimum.\n\nThe actual initialization obtained below is\n\n    z_0=q_t+(S+t)p\n       =(((1+(S+t)T)/M) U_0, -R).                      (6.2)\n\nThus Law II respects the requested initial vertical slice exactly.\nNo vertical drift is suppressed or absorbed into an unpaid carrier.\nThe original supplied z_star=(0,-R) remains a minimizer, but q_t,\nrather than z_star, will be the closest minimizer.\n\nFor a generic y=q_t+A p with A>=0 and any h>1, comparison of its\ninverse scores with p gives\n\n    score(v_j)-score(p)\n      =<q_t,v_j> + A(<p,v_j>-T)\n         +(h-1)(||v_j||^2-T)/2 <0, j!=0.               (6.3)\n\nHere <p,v_j><=||p||||v_j||<T and ||v_j||^2<T; all signs follow from\nthe strict maximum radius. For the zero vertex the difference is\n-(A+(h-1)/2)T<0. Therefore the inverse rule chooses p uniquely for\nEVERY original step, beginning at y_t. The actual reverse construction\nhas the closed form\n\n    A_i=t+sum_(k=i)^(n-1)h_k,\n    z_i=q_t+A_i p, 0<=i<=n.                            (6.4)\n\nThis is not left as a recurrence. For i<n, A_i>=h_(n-1)>1.\nConsequently every queried gradient is p, which can also be checked\ndirectly: for r_i=q_t+(A_i-1)p, every original vertex w obeys\n\n    <r_i,w-p>=<q_t,w>+(A_i-1)(<p,w>-T)<=0.              (6.5)\n\nThe vertical coordinate is the actual physical one:\n\n    (z_i)_vertical=-R-(sum_(k<i)h_k)/R,\n\nand each step changes it by exactly -h_i/R, the required vertical\ngradient component of -h_i p. (6.6)\n\nFor 0<=t<=1, this point belongs to the edge [0,p], and the residual\nis q_t, which is normal to D and perpendicular to p. For t>=1,\n(6.5) with A_i=t proves projection p. Thus every original query,\nthe actual terminal projection, and every face-boundary case are fixed\nby the single body. Use accepted interpolation and benchmark facts only at their\nreal scopes; jointly derive any prior-draft premise.\n\nWrite RESULT.md and HANDOFF.md in this new public directory with complete\nall-m parameter scopes, exact counterdirections/cycles if rejected, and\nresource counts. Stop this ONE allocation on return. No publication,\nfinalization, revocation, shutdown, partial-result Goal completion or renewal.\n\n\n---\n\n# Correlated-character repair still needs strict short-step margins\n\nMAIN-AUTHORED UNVERIFIED derivation. Original target OPEN. No program was used.\nThis is not part of the completed native two-law allocation.\n\nUse the SAME m>=1536 hierarchy, N=2^m, K=N-1-m,\nbeta=1-1/(4m), a=beta/[(1+sqrt2)(1+1/(4m))].\nLet dominant character labels be tau_k=N-2^k,0<=k<m, with weights\nb_(tau_k)^2=2^k/(N-1). Give every remaining nonzero character weight\nzeta/(N^2 K),0<zeta<=1. All characters remain present. Set\nU_s=(b_t chi_t(s))_(t!=0), M=sum b_t^2=1+zeta/N^2,\nT=M+R^(-2), w_s=(U_s,1/R), R>0.\n\nIf Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2), then\nchi_(tau_k)(Gamma(i))=(-1)^(bit_k(i)), because the xor sum of Gray bits\nk through m-1 telescopes to bit_k(i). No chronology is permuted.\nFor a flip of bit l the exact marginal energy is\n\n E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)\n       +zeta*(N/2-l-1)/(N^2 K).\n\nThe joint energy for bits0 and1 is\n J=1/(N-1)+zeta*(N/4-1)/(N^2 K),\n E_1-2J=1/(N-1).                                    (1)\n\nThe improved overlap may fix the old first triple, but it does not repair\nthe undamped short-step root law. Consider ORIGINAL queries1,2,3, whose\ntwo physical steps are1+beta and1+a and whose Gray bit flips are1 then0.\nLet u be the positive adjacent firm-equality radius root at the first\nstep and v the corresponding root at the second; both lie in(0,1).\nWrite e=E_1,f=E_0, and retain the positive overall initial radius scale s.\nBoth adjacent equality conditions force both corresponding projection\nnormal rows to be0. The remote query-3 normal against the query-1 vertex\nis then exactly\n\n B=s^2*u*[(a+v)A+2v f(1-u)-4vJ],\n A=T(u-1)+2e=2e*beta*(1+u)/(u+beta)>2e*beta.         (2)\n\nTo rederive(2), use three unscaled frame vectors W0,W1,W2 with inner\nproducts T-2e,T-2f,T-2e-2f+4J. Set g0=s W0,g1=s u W1,g2=s u v W2.\nAt the middle point the residual is orthogonal to both differences.\nThe last residual differs by -a*g1-g2. Its normal against g0 is\n(a*g1+g2) dot(g2-g0), which expands to(2), using the second root\nequality. The first root equation rearranges as\n(1-u)(u+beta)=2(1-beta)e*u/T, yielding the expression for A.\n\nEvery omitted factor is positive, so B>s^2*u*v*(2beta E_1-4J).\nEquation(1) gives\n\n 2beta E_1-4J=2/(N-1)-2(1-beta)E_1>0.\n\nIndeed K>=4 and zeta<=1 imply E_1<4/(N-1), while1-beta<=1/6144.\nThus B>0 for every declared R,zeta,m. This is a uniform original-row\ncontradiction, independent of every later radius choice and the initial\npoint. It applies only when BOTH low-level radius ratios retain their\nadjacent equality roots. It does not exclude strict short-step damping,\ndifferent overlaps or a different actual itinerary.\n\nThe next proposed law must therefore alter at least one of these low-level\nequalities, rather than claim that correlated characters alone fixed the\nglobal constraints. Feasibility and payoff of a damped law remain OPEN.\n", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["98e97399b140356f", "a753ad89e79ede74"], "status": "fresh_cap1_active", "native_agent": "/root/direct_gray_radial_vertices", "allocation": "research/direct_correlated_gray_damping_20261003/ALLOCATION.json", "formal_calls": 0, "original_target": "OPEN", "artifact": "research/direct_correlated_gray_damping_20261003/TASK.md", "bytes": 4331, "sha256": "49a32b3d3acf987e13f0fe293295299006f938af4a5d1e8430adffcdb73beabc"}, "glossary": {}}

{"id": "fa3df3d15eb42d77", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T20:16:13.563285+00:00", "author": "main_agent", "kind": "master_guidance", "claim": "SELF-AUTHORED main FULL-read26392-byte native strict Gray report: JOINT UNVERIFIED necessary screens R^-2<M and theta<48/(floor(m/2)-2), exact terminal radius product>exp(-16); small-theta nonacute feasibility/true-radius payoff remains OPEN. ONE-law allocation complete, no second mechanism or formal/numerical call.", "evidence": "# Strict-margin correlated Gray radii: two uniform necessary screens, remaining law unresolved\n\n**JOINT UNVERIFIED RESULT.** Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`.\nThis is the complete report of ONE DISTINCT bounded analytical allocation,\nafter the completed `direct_gray_radial_vertices_20261003` allocation.\nExactly ONE new substantive mechanism was examined: the specified strictly\ndamped correlated-character radial itinerary. No second mechanism was\nbegun. The first law has NOT been completely excluded or made into a\nlegal quantitative witness. The new scoped conclusions below are joint\ndraft derivations, not verifier-accepted facts.\n\nThe new results are as follows.\n\n1. The task's undamped correlated-character screen is confirmed jointly:\n   original queries 1,2,3 are inconsistent if both short-level ratios are\n   their firm-equality roots. This does not transfer to strict damping.\n2. For the strictly damped law, the entire parameter domain R^(-2)>=M\n   is impossible, by summing all ORIGINAL zero-vertex normal rows.\n3. Independently of the initialization, a remote ORIGINAL firm pair at\n   query indices 2^j-1 and 2^(j+1)-1, j=floor(m/2), is inconsistent\n   whenever sigma_(2^j)>=1. In the remaining domain R^(-2)<M, an explicit\n   all-m estimate makes this a parameter exclusion:\n\n       theta (floor(m/2)-2)>=48  ==>  no prescribed trajectory.\n\n   Thus any selection legal for every m would necessarily have\n   theta_m<48/(floor(m/2)-2), not a fixed positive theta.\n4. The total added damping is exactly a product bounded below by a\n   positive absolute constant. In fact the entire terminal radius ratio\n   satisfies sigma_n>exp(-16) throughout the declared parameter family.\n   Counting the short steps does NOT prove exponential loss in m or N.\n\nWhat remains is genuine: 0<R^(-2)<M and\n0<=theta<48/(floor(m/2)-2), with both positive damping parameters allowed,\nhave not been made feasible or excluded. No explicit legal x_0, exact\nclosest-radius terminal lower bound, or gain over the COMPLETE channel\nhas been proved there. Ending this bounded allocation is not a claim\nthat the requested mathematical search objective was achieved.\n\n## 1. Preserved original target and declared parameters\n\nThe original minimax problem remains\n\n    W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i),\n    W_n^star=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h), W=2R,\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n=1/(2S_bullet(n)+1).\n\nThe affirmative target is W_n^star/U_n^ZJ -> 1, with one threshold for\nEVERY sufficiently large n and EVERY predetermined nonnegative word of\nlength n. A negative resolution requires a full-function upper\ncertificate proving a nonvanishing separation on an unbounded\nsubsequence. Neither restricted witness infeasibility nor an attained\nwitness score is that certificate. The complete original PROBLEM.md\nwas read and is unchanged. Its integer-split benchmark and original\nnormalization are not replaced.\n\nFix the SAME m>=1536 in every subword and define\n\n    N=2^m, n=N-1, eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,\n    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    H_l=1+a c^l, h_i=H_(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n,\n    S=sum h_i, D_m=1+2S.\n\nElementary bounds used below are\n\n    0<eps<1/100, beta>99/100, 2<c<5/2,\n    1/3<a<1/2, H_0=1+a<2, H_1=1+beta<2,\n    H_l>2 for l>=2, H_l>6 for l>=4.\n\nFor example 1+sqrt(2)<49/20 and 1+eps<101/100 prove c<5/2;\na>99/250>1/3. Also a c^2=beta c>1, while for l>=4,\na c^l>2^l/3>=16/3, giving the claimed signs.\n\nThe strict law fixes parameters BEFORE the run:\n\n    R>0, 0<zeta<=1, 0<delta_0,delta_1<=16, 0<=theta<=1,\n\npossibly as explicit functions of m. Since N>16, all factors\n1-delta_l/N are strictly positive. No parameter is fit to gradients\nobserved during the run.\n\nThe COMPLETE K/cut reference contains the full-word Huber term 1/D_m;\nthe task-supplied accepted comparison B_n/D_m<47/50 is an UPPER\ncomparison. No lower gain is inferred by reversing it. In fact no use\nof that comparison is required for any new conclusion in this report.\n\n## 2. Correlated characters, real metric, and one static body\n\nFor each k=0,...,m-1 let tau_k=N-2^k, the character label whose bits\nk through m-1 are 1. Let Kmix=N-1-m, and for all nonzero character\nlabels t in F_2^m define\n\n    b_(tau_k)^2=2^k/(N-1),\n    b_t^2=zeta/(N^2 Kmix) for the remaining labels,\n    U_s(t)=b_t (-1)^(t dot s).\n\nThere are exactly m distinct dominant labels. Every other nonzero\nlabel remains present with strictly positive weight. Let\n\n    M=sum_(t!=0)b_t^2=1+zeta/N^2,\n    T=M+R^(-2), w_s=(U_s,1/R).\n\nThese are real Euclidean vectors, with the vertical contribution fully\nincluded. Walsh orthogonality follows by pairing labels differing in\none bit:\n\n    sum_s chi_t(s)=0 for nonzero t,\n    sum_s chi_t(s)chi_u(s)=N*1_(t=u).\n\nIncluding the constant row proves the N by N full Walsh matrix is\ninvertible. Thus the N vectors w_s are linearly independent, as all\ncoordinate weights and 1/R are positive. Positive radial rescaling\npreserves independence. With Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2), set\n\n    v_i=sigma_i w_(Gamma(i)), 0<=i<=n,\n    D=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}, C=cone{v_0,...,v_n},\n    F_D(z)=max_(g in D) {<z,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nThis is one full-dimensional simplex in R^N, not an expected Gram or\na changing collection of functions. No universality representation is\nused as a proof premise. The cone is independent of the positive\nradii and is closed because it is an invertible linear image of the\nnonnegative orthant. Its full polar is\n\n    C^polar={(x,y): <x,U_s>+y/R<=0 for EVERY original s}.\n\nCompleting the square makes the unique maximizer P_D(z). Its exact\ncharacterization is\n\n    g in D, <z-g,w-g><=0 for EVERY vertex w of D, including 0. (2.1)\n\nThe usual two-normal addition directly gives\n\n    <z-z',P_D(z)-P_D(z')> >= ||P_D(z)-P_D(z')||^2.       (2.2)\n\nComparing the two maximizing affine expressions gives differentiability\nwith gradient P_D; (2.2) gives 1-Lipschitz continuity. F_D is finite\nand convex, and on the open interior of D it equals ||z||^2/2, so its\nleast Lipschitz gradient constant is exactly 1.\n\nThe FULL minimum is\n\n    argmin F_D=C^polar, min F_D=0.                       (2.3)\n\nIndeed the value is zero on the polar. Conversely, if <z,v_i>>0,\nthen a sufficiently small positive multiple of v_i in D makes the\ndefining objective strictly positive. In particular z_star=(0,-R)\nis a strict polar point, with <z_star,v_i>=-sigma_i. For the required\nslice z_0=(x_0,-R), this particular minimum has distance ||x_0||;\nthe closest minimum may be different. Multiplying F_D by L>0 gives\nthe same physical GD run and the same W=2R score under step h_i/L.\n\n## 3. Physical ordering and exact overlap identities\n\nWriting the binary bits of i as i_k, Gray bit l is i_l xor i_(l+1),\nwhere i_m=0. The xor sum of Gray bits k through m-1 telescopes, hence\n\n    chi_(tau_k)(Gamma(i))=(-1)^(i_k).                   (3.1)\n\nThus the dominant physical coordinates run in binary-counter order;\nthe actual Gray labels have NOT been chronologically permuted. Also,\nif l=nu_2(i+1), incrementing i changes bits 0 through l, whose Gray\nchanges cancel except at l. Therefore\n\n    Gamma(i+1)=Gamma(i)+e_l.\n\nAmong all characters, N/2 have bit l set; among the dominant labels,\nexactly tau_0,...,tau_l do. The exact flip energies are consequently\n\n    E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)\n          +zeta (N/2-l-1)/(N^2 Kmix).                   (3.2)\n\nFor the intersection of physical bits 0 and 1, the only dominant label\nis tau_0. Of all characters exactly N/4 have both bits set. Thus\n\n    J=1/(N-1)+zeta (N/4-1)/(N^2 Kmix),\n    E_1-2J=1/(N-1).                                    (3.3)\n\nFor any labels s,u let E(s+u) denote the sum of b_t^2 on characters\nwith t dot (s+u)=1. Then\n\n    <w_s,w_u>=T-2E(s+u), ||w_s||^2=T.                  (3.4)\n\nFor three successive frames with flips l then k, their pairwise\ninner products are T-2E_l, T-2E_k, and\nT-2E_l-2E_k+4J_lk. This includes the vertical component in every row.\n\n## 4. Adjacent roots, the jointly checked undamped screen, and strictness\n\nLet t_l=1-2E_l/T. Since 0<E_l<M<T, -1<t_l<1. If a physical step\nH_l connects projected gradients p=s w and q=s r w', (2.2), or the\ntwo original normal rows, requires\n\n    r^2+(H_l-2)t_l r+1-H_l<=0.                          (4.1)\n\nIts unique positive root is\n\n    rho_l=[-(H_l-2)t_l+\n        sqrt((H_l-2)^2 t_l^2+4(H_l-1))]/2.\n\nSince the polynomial at 1 equals (2-H_l)(1-t_l), one has\n0<rho_l<1 at l=0,1 and rho_l>1 at all l>=2.\nAt equality, the adjacent residuals are both perpendicular to the\ngradient difference: subtracting the residuals gives (H_l-1)p+q,\nwhose inner product with q-p is zero; the two original normals have\nopposite required signs and therefore must both be zero.\n\nHere is a joint verification of the task's UNVERIFIED repair screen,\nused only as motivation for damping. Original queries 1,2,3 have\nphysical steps H_1 then H_0 and character flips 1 then 0. Suppose both\nratios are their roots. Put u=rho_1, v=rho_0, e=E_1, f=E_0, and let\ns>0 be the overall starting scale at query 1. Write the gradients as\np_0=sW_0, p_1=suW_1, p_2=suvW_2. The middle residual is perpendicular\nto both differences. The final original normal against p_0 is forced\nto equal\n\n    B=s^2 u[(a+v)A+2vf(1-u)-4vJ],\n    A=T(u-1)+2e=2e beta(1+u)/(u+beta)>2e beta.           (4.2)\n\nTo check this, use the three inner products in (3.4), the last\nresidual difference -a p_1-p_2, and the second root equality to replace\n(a p_1+p_2) dot (p_2-p_0) by its dot product with p_1-p_0. Expanding\ngives the first expression. The first root equation rearranges as\n(1-u)(u+beta)=2(1-beta)e u/T, giving the second expression.\n\nAll other displayed contributions are positive, so\n\n    B>s^2uv(2beta E_1-4J)\n     =s^2uv[2/(N-1)-2eps E_1]>0.\n\nThe last sign follows from E_1<4/(N-1), Kmix>=4, zeta<=1 and\neps<=1/6144. This contradicts the original query-3 normal against\nthe original query-1 vertex. This reconfirms the screen WITHOUT\nadopting it as an accepted fact.\n\nThe actual NEW substantive law is instead\n\n    r_l=rho_l(1-delta_l/N), l=0,1,\n    r_l=1+theta(rho_l-1), 2<=l<m,\n    sigma_0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i r_(nu_2(i+1)).         (4.3)\n\nBoth low ratios are strictly between 0 and rho_l, so (4.1) is now\nstrict at those levels. The two orthogonality equalities used in\n(4.2) are lost. No claim that (4.2) remains a contradiction under\narbitrary strict damping is made. All higher ratios belong to\n[1,rho_l], so every local adjacent necessary inequality holds.\nThis local fact is not a whole-word construction.\n\n## 5. Complete exclusion of the acute-generator parameter domain\n\n**Screen A, joint draft.** For every m>=1536 and every strict-law\nparameter, if R^(-2)>=M, there is no prescribed trajectory from\nz_0=(x_0,-R) having the vertices v_0,...,v_n as all original\nprojections, including the terminal one. This statement in fact\nholds for arbitrary positive radii in this full dictionary.\n\nFor any two frames, Cauchy-Schwarz on their horizontal coordinates\ngives\n\n    <w_s,w_u>=<U_s,U_u>+R^(-2)>=-M+R^(-2)>=0.\n\nIf the prescribed run existed, put\nP_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k, so z_i=z_0-P_i. Its ORIGINAL normal against\nthe zero vertex is\n\n    <z_i-v_i,v_i>>=0.\n\nDivide by the positive sigma_i and substitute the run:\n\n    <z_0,w_(Gamma(i))>\n       >=<P_i,w_(Gamma(i))>+sigma_i T>0,                (5.1)\n\nbecause all h_k are positive and all frame Gram entries are\nnonnegative. Sum (5.1) over every original i=0,...,n. Gamma is a\nbijection and the nontrivial Walsh rows sum to zero, so\n\n    sum_i <z_0,w_(Gamma(i))>\n      =<(x_0,-R),(0,N/R)>=-N.\n\nThis contradicts its strict positivity. The contradiction is an\nexplicit sum of original zero-vertex normal rows, with the positive\nweights 1/sigma_i. It uses no fitted function values or numerical\nfeasibility calculation. The terminal row is included; no claim is\nmade about an itinerary that omits that prescribed terminal vertex.\n\nHence the only surviving geometric domain is\n\n    0<R^(-2)<M, 1<T<2M<9/4.                            (5.2)\n\n## 6. Whole-word radius product: no exponential-loss shortcut\n\nThe number of transitions at level l among the n=N-1 original steps\nis exactly N/2^(l+1). Thus the full terminal radius ratio is\n\n    sigma_n=rho_0^(N/2) rho_1^(N/4)\n       * product_(l=2)^(m-1) [1+theta(rho_l-1)]^(N/2^(l+1))\n       * D_add,\n    D_add=(1-delta_0/N)^(N/2)(1-delta_1/N)^(N/4).        (6.1)\n\nFor 0<=x<1, integration of 1/(1-u) over [0,x] gives\n\n    -x/(1-x)<=log(1-x)<=-x.\n\nConsequently the EXACT extra damping factor obeys\n\n    exp[-(delta_0/2+delta_1/4)/(1-16/N)]\n       <=D_add<=exp[-delta_0/2-delta_1/4].               (6.2)\n\nAs N>=512, the lower exponent is greater than -13. This is a\npositive absolute lower bound, even when both delta parameters depend\non m. The short-step count does not give a loss tending to zero.\n\nFor later bounds, direct counting in (3.2) gives E_0<2/N and E_1<4/N.\nFor a short step H=1+d, its root identity is\n\n    (1-rho)(rho+d)=2(1-d)E rho/T.\n\nSince T>1, this yields 1-rho_0<=4/N and\n1-rho_1<=8eps/N. The logarithmic inequality above gives, for the\npresent N,\n\n    log rho_0>=-5/N, log rho_1>=-9eps/N.                (6.3)\n\nThe first uses 4/(N-4)<=5/N, and the second uses\n8eps/(N-8eps)<=9eps/N; both hold uniformly here. Thus the product of\nthe two undamped short-root contributions is greater than exp(-3).\nEvery higher-level contribution in (6.1) is at least 1. Combining\nthese observations proves the all-parameter bound\n\n    sigma_n>exp(-16).                                  (6.4)\n\nThis is only a statement about the prescribed radii. It does not\nassert the trajectory exists or that its objective score has a\npositive asymptotic gain; the initial true distance has not been paid.\n\n## 7. Exact dyadic self-similarity and prefix estimates\n\nPut\n\n    j=floor(m/2), L=2^j, Q=sigma_L, s=sigma_(L-1).\n\nThus j>=768 and 2L-1 is an original query index strictly inside\nthe full horizon. No child resets m. For any integer i>=0, the count\nof transitions of level l before query i is\n\n    c_l(i)=floor(i/2^l)-floor(i/2^(l+1)).\n\nFor 0<=i<L this gives\n\n    sigma_(L+i)=Q sigma_i,\n    Q=r_j product_(l=0)^(j-1) r_l^(L/2^(l+1)).          (7.1)\n\nIndeed c_l(L+i)=L/2^(l+1)+c_l(i) for l<j, while the level-j\ncount is 1 and all higher counts are zero. Also L+i=L xor i, and\nthe Gray map is linear over bitwise xor. Let B be the real diagonal\nsign map associated with Gamma(L)=e_j+e_(j-1), leaving the vertical\ncoordinate fixed. Then\n\n    B^2=I, B^T=B,\n    w_(Gamma(L+i))=B w_(Gamma(i)),\n    v_(L+i)=Q B v_i.                                   (7.2)\n\nBy (3.1), B flips exactly the dominant coordinate tau_j and leaves\nall other dominant coordinates unchanged. Some mixed coordinates\nalso flip. Their entire squared energy is at most zeta/N^2.\n\nHere are explicit UNIFORM estimates for every prefix index 0<=i<=L.\nFor l<=j, (3.2) gives E_l<=3*2^l/N. Since L/N<=2^(-j),\nE_l<1/4 and t_l>=1/2. At a high level, substituting 1/t_l into the\nroot polynomial gives 1/t_l^2-1>=0, so\n\n    1<=r_l<=rho_l<=1/t_l,\n    rho_l-1<=4E_l<=12*2^l/N, 2<=l<=j.                 (7.3)\n\nIn particular rho_l<=9/8. For the short levels, (6.3), and\ndelta/(N-delta)<=17/N for delta<=16 and N>=272, imply\n\n    log r_0>=-22/N,\n    log r_1>=-(17+9eps)/N>-18/N.                       (7.4)\n\nAll high-level logarithms are nonnegative. Counts up to i<=L do not\nexceed the counts at L. Equations (7.3)-(7.4) therefore give\n\n    -16 L/N <= log sigma_i <= 6(j+1)L/N.               (7.5)\n\nFor the lower bound the two short contributions are bounded by\n-(22/2+18/4)L/N=-(31/2)L/N>-16L/N. For the upper bound each level\n2<=l<j contributes at most 6L/N and level j at most 12L/N; the\ndisplayed coarser bound also covers small prefix indices.\n\nFor completeness, 2^j>=j^2 for j>=4 follows by induction from\n2j^2>=(j+1)^2. Since j>=768, (7.5) implies\n\n    -1/16<=log sigma_i<=1/16,\n    sigma_i/s>=exp(-1/8)>=7/8, 0<=i<=L.                (7.6)\n\nFor example 6(j+1)/2^j<=7/j<1/16, and\n16/2^j<=16/j^2<1/16. These are symbolic all-j estimates, not an\nexecuted trajectory or a finite horizon check.\n\n## 8. A remote ORIGINAL firm-pair obstruction when Q>=1\n\n**Screen B, joint draft.** For the full strict-law family, if\nQ=sigma_(2^floor(m/2))>=1, there is no prescribed original trajectory,\nfrom ANY initial point. No initial-slice assumption is needed for this\nscreen. The violated condition uses the two original query indices\nL-1 and 2L-1, far apart in physical time.\n\nLet p=v_(L-1) and decompose every vector into B's orthogonal eigenspaces,\nv=v^++v^- with Bv^+=v^+, Bv^-=-v^-. Let\n\n    P=sum_(i=0)^(L-2) h_i v_i,\n    S_j=sum_(i=0)^(L-2)h_i,\n    d_j=a c^j, H_j=1+d_j.\n\nThe original word between the two stated query indices is its level-j\nbridge followed by one whole child of length L-1. Equations (7.1)-(7.2)\ntherefore give the EXACT original gradient and displacement\n\n    v_(2L-1)=Q Bp,\n    z_(L-1)-z_(2L-1)=H_j p+Q BP.                       (8.1)\n\nNo chronological permutation or omitted last child leaf occurs in\n(8.1). In particular P includes all the L-1 steps of that child,\nincluding its actual final H_0 leaf.\n\nThe firm condition (2.2) for these two original points requires the\nfollowing explicitly computed quantity to be NONNEGATIVE:\n\n    F=<H_j p+Q BP, p-Q Bp>-||p-Q Bp||^2\n     =(1-Q)[(H_j-1+Q)||p^+||^2+Q<P^+,p^+>]\n       +(1+Q)[(H_j-1-Q)||p^-||^2-Q<P^-,p^->].          (8.2)\n\nWe show that it is strictly negative whenever Q>=1.\n\nFirst, <P^+,p^+>>0. All binary labels in the first block have bit j\nand every higher bit zero. In B's plus space, the dominant coordinates\nabove j have common positive signs, those below j have arbitrary\nproducts, and the total mixed energy is at most zeta/N^2. Therefore\nfor every 0<=i<L,\n\n    <w_(Gamma(i))^+,w_(Gamma(L-1))^+>\n       >=R^(-2)+(N-3L+1)/(N-1)-zeta/N^2>0.             (8.3)\n\nThe last sign follows from N>=L^2, L>=4 and zeta<=1; for instance\n3L<=3N/4 and the positive dominant term is larger than 1/4, whereas\n1/N^2<1/4. Multiplying by positive h_i sigma_i s proves the assertion.\n\nSecond, the minus-space ratio is bounded BELOW. Its only dominant\ncoordinate is tau_j, with squared weight\n\n    b_j^2=L/(N-1).\n\nAll first-block vectors have the same positive sign in that coordinate.\nPut eta=zeta/N^2. Then\n\n    ||p^-||^2<=s^2(b_j^2+eta),\n    <P^-,p^->>=s (b_j^2-eta) sum_(i=0)^(L-2)h_i sigma_i.\n\nHere eta/b_j^2<=1/N. Hence, using (7.6) and N>=15,\n\n    <P^-,p^->/||p^-||^2\n      >=[(1-1/N)/(1+1/N)] (7/8) S_j\n      >=(49/64)S_j > (3/4)S_j.                        (8.4)\n\nThird, the bridge is smaller than (3/4)S_j. Exact counting of levels\nin the child gives\n\n    S_j=(2^j-1)+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2)\n       =[a/(c-2)]c^j+[1-a/(c-2)]2^j-1.\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2). Since lambda(lambda-2)=1,\nc>lambda and beta<1 imply a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)]<1.\nThus S_j>d_j/(c-2)-1>2d_j-1. Also d_j>2^j/3>7/2,\nso\n\n    H_j=1+d_j < (3/4)(2d_j-1) < (3/4)S_j.             (8.5)\n\nNow if Q>=1, the first term of (8.2) is nonpositive by (8.3).\nIts second bracket, divided by the positive ||p^-||^2, is\n\n    H_j-1-Q-Q<P^-,p^->/||p^-||^2\n       <H_j-Q(3/4)S_j<0\n\nby (8.4)-(8.5). The second term is strictly negative. Hence F<0,\ncontradicting the original firm pair. This proves Screen B.\n\nThis obstruction differs from a root-saturated three-query argument:\nboth low-level inequalities may be strict, the compared queries are\nremote, and the derivation never sets an adjacent normal slack to zero.\n\n## 9. An explicit parameter exclusion: theta must be O(1/m)\n\nIt remains to turn Q>=1 into a proved all-parameter subdomain,\nwithout computing Q. Work in the surviving geometric domain (5.2),\nso T<9/4. For 4<=l<=j, H_l>6, and subtracting the root polynomial\nat 1 from its value at rho_l gives\n\n    (rho_l-1)[rho_l+1+(H_l-2)t_l]=2(H_l-2)E_l/T.\n\nAs rho_l<=9/8<2 and t_l<=1, the bracket is at most H_l+1.\nSince (H_l-2)/(H_l+1)>4/7 and T<9/4, it follows that\n\n    rho_l-1 >= (32/63) E_l.\n\nFor 1<=r_l<=9/8, log r_l>= (r_l-1)/r_l. Thus\n\n    log r_l >= (8/9)theta(rho_l-1)\n       >= (256/567)theta E_l\n       > (4/9)theta E_l\n       >= (2/3)theta 2^l/N, 4<=l<=j.                  (9.1)\n\nAt theta=0 replace the one strict inequality by equality; the weak\nconclusion remains valid. The final bound uses\nE_l>=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)>=(3/2)2^l/N for l>=1.\n\nInsert these bounds into (7.1), keep the high levels 2 and 3 merely\nnonnegative, and use (7.4) for the two negative short terms. The\nlevels 4,...,j-1 each contribute at least theta L/(3N), while\nlevel j contributes at least 2theta L/(3N). Consequently\n\n    log Q >= (L/N)[theta(j-2)/3-16].                   (9.2)\n\nIn particular theta(j-2)>=48 implies Q>=1 and hence Screen B.\nTogether with Screen A, this proves the following exact necessary\nparameter restrictions for the whole prescribed trajectory:\n\n    R^(-2)<M,\n    theta < 48/(floor(m/2)-2).                         (9.3)\n\nThe inequalities are strict: their equality boundaries are excluded\nby the preceding contradictions. Parameters delta_0,delta_1 may be\nanywhere in (0,16] and may depend explicitly on m; the proof is uniform\nover all such choices. For any fixed theta>0, every sufficiently large\nm violates the second necessary condition. Parameters of order 1/m,\nincluding theta=0, are NOT excluded by this argument.\n\nEquation (9.3) is a scoped obstruction, not a complete answer to the\nstrict-law task. In particular it does not justify setting theta=0\nand declaring the remaining normals feasible.\n\n## 10. Actual rows, vertical price, and the unclosed performance interface\n\nFor any legal projection trajectory of the single F_D, set\n\n    g_i=P_D(z_i), f_i=<z_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,\n    r_i=z_i-g_i.\n\nExpansion and the original normal tests give every ordered row,\nincluding both terminal directions:\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.\n\nFor any minimizer q in the FULL polar cone, with gradient and value\nzero, the two rows are Q_(i,star)=<g_i,q><=0 and\nQ_(star,i)=-<r_i,g_i><=0. The real Gram is PSD by its construction.\nThese facts do not supply the missing z_0 that makes the prescribed\nv_i equal the actual g_i; that assertion remains unproved in (9.3).\n\nIf that assertion were established, the exact original run would have\n\n    P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k,\n    z_i=(x_0,-R)-P_i,\n    (z_i)_vertical=-R-R^(-1)sum_(k<i)h_k sigma_k.        (10.1)\n\nThus the changing vertical gradient is fully paid. Any candidate\nthat keeps the vertical coordinate fixed is not the required GD run.\nThe zero-vertex normal height <z_i-v_i,v_i> and slack relative to a\nnonzero chosen polar minimum are different quantities. No claim of\ncap-active legality is made merely from the latter slack.\n\nThe own closest initial radius is\n\n    R_init=dist((x_0,-R),C^polar),\n\nnot automatically ||x_0||. The general elementary bounds, if the first\nprescribed query is legal, are sqrt(T)<=R_init<=||x_0||: the upper\nbound uses z_star=(0,-R), and the lower uses the 1-Lipschitz property\nof P_D between z_0 and any minimizer. Neither bound pays the unknown\ninitialization sharply enough for a terminal gain. If the terminal\nprojection is indeed v_n, its exact score would be\n\n    E=[2<(x_0,-R),v_n>-2 sum_(i<n)h_i<v_i,v_n>-||v_n||^2]\n        /R_init^2. The original PROBLEM.md,\nall-integer-split benchmark, and normalization are unchanged.\n\nKeep the SAME enclosing integer m>=1536 in every child and set\n\n    N=2^m, n=N-1, eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,\n    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    H_l=1+a c^l, h_i=H_(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n,\n    S=sum_(i=0)^(n-1)h_i, D_m=1+2S.\n\nThe fixed parameters, permitted to depend explicitly on m before the run,\nare R>0, 0<zeta<=1, 0<delta_0,delta_1<=16 and 0<=theta<=1.\nElementary bounds used below are\n\n    beta>999/1000, 2<c<49/20<5/2,\n    2/5<a<1/2, H_0=1+a<2, H_1=1+beta<2,\n    H_l>2 for l>=2.\n\nIndeed eps<=1/6144<1/1000 and 1+sqrt(2)<17/7 imply\nc<(17/7)(1001/1000)<49/20. Thus\na>(999/1000)/(49/20)=999/2450>2/5. All inequalities are\nsymbolic uniform estimates, not a numerical replay.\n\nLet tau_k=N-2^k, Kmix=N-1-m, and retain every nonzero character\nt in F_2^m with positive weight\n\n    b_(tau_k)^2=2^k/(N-1), 0<=k<m,\n    b_t^2=zeta/(N^2 Kmix) on the remaining labels,\n    U_s(t)=b_t(-1)^(t dot s),\n    M=sum b_t^2=1+zeta/N^2, T=M+R^(-2),\n    w_s=(U_s,1/R), Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2).\n\nWrite w_i for w_(Gamma(i)) only when i is an original time index.\nThe actual proposed vertices and static body are\n\n    v_i=sigma_i w_i, D=conv(0,v_0,...,v_n),\n    F_D(z)=max_(g in D) [<z,g>-||g||^2/2].\n\nFor a physical Gray flip of bit l the exact marginal energy and cosine\nare\n\n    E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)\n          +zeta(N/2-l-1)/(N^2 Kmix),\n    t_l=1-2E_l/T.\n\nThe positive root rho_l solves\n\n    rho_l^2+(H_l-2)t_l rho_l+1-H_l=0.\n\nThe law under examination is\n\n    r_l=rho_l(1-delta_l/N), l=0,1,\n    r_l=1+theta(rho_l-1), 2<=l<m,\n    sigma_0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i r_(nu_2(i+1)).        (1.1)\n\nBecause N>16 all ratios are positive. The root polynomial at 1 is\n(2-H_l)(1-t_l), so 0<rho_l<1 for l=0,1 and rho_l>1 otherwise.\nThus the low ratios are strictly below their roots, while the high\nratios are between 1 and rho_l. All local necessary firm inequalities\nhold. No global feasibility is inferred from this.\n\n## 2. Needed geometry and all-original-row conventions, derived jointly\n\nGray bit l equals i_l xor i_(l+1). Xoring bits k through m-1 telescopes,\nso chi_(tau_k)(Gamma(i))=(-1)^(i_k). Incrementing i flips Gray bit\nnu_2(i+1), with no permutation of chronological samples. Among the\ncharacters with bit l set, exactly l+1 are dominant; this proves the\nformula for E_l above. For the overlap of bits 0 and 1,\n\n    J=1/(N-1)+zeta(N/4-1)/(N^2 Kmix),\n    E_1-2J=1/(N-1).                                   (2.1)\n\nEvery frame has squared norm T, and\n\n    <w_s,w_u>=T-2E(s+u),\n\nwhere E(d) sums weights of all characters changing sign under d.\nWalsh orthogonality, proved by pairing group elements differing in one\nbit, gives sum_s U_s=0 and makes the full Walsh matrix invertible.\nThe N vectors w_s, and hence the positively rescaled v_i, are linearly\nindependent. D is a full-dimensional simplex in R^N including vertex 0.\n\nCompleting the square identifies the unique maximizer of F_D as P_D(z).\nThe exact projection conditions are\n\n    g in D, <z-g,w-g><=0 for EVERY original vertex w, including 0. (2.2)\n\nExpanding squared distance proves sufficiency; differentiation along\neach segment proves necessity. Adding the two normal inequalities at\ntwo points proves firm nonexpansiveness and thus 1-Lipschitz continuity\nof P_D. Comparison of the maximizing affine expressions proves that\nF_D is differentiable with gradient P_D. It is finite and convex; on\nthe interior of D it equals ||z||^2/2, so its least smoothness constant\nis exactly 1.\n\nThe complete minimum is the polar of the closed cone generated by D:\n\n    C=cone{v_i},\n    argmin F_D=C^polar\n       ={(x,y): <x,U_s>+y/R<=0 for EVERY s}, min F_D=0.   (2.3)\n\nIndeed 0 gives value 0, every polar point has value 0, and any positive\ndot product with a generator gives a positive value on a sufficiently\nshort segment from 0 to that generator. The cone is closed because it\nis an invertible linear image of the nonnegative orthant. Its polar is\nindependent of the positive radii. The specified z_star=(0,-R) is a\nstrict polar minimizer, with dot product -sigma_i against v_i.\n\nFor any ACTUAL run, let g_i=P_D(z_i), r_i=z_i-g_i and\nf_i=<z_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Its original ordered interpolation rows are\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                            (2.4)\n\nFor any polar minimizer q with gradient/value 0,\nQ_(i,star)=<g_i,q><=0 and Q_(star,i)=-<r_i,g_i><=0.\nIn particular the zero-vertex row requires\n\n    f_i>=||g_i||^2/2.                                 (2.5)\n\nThese facts are derived from one body. No prior interpolation draft,\nuniversality result, or numerical Gram is adopted as a premise.\nMultiplication of F_D by L gives the original L-smooth normalization\nand leaves the physical iterates and W=2R payoff unchanged.\n\nWe also rederive a useful whole-domain screen. If R^(-2)>=M, then\nall frame Gram entries are nonnegative. For a prescribed run from\nz_0=(x_0,-R), put P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t v_t. The original zero-vertex\nnormal, divided by sigma_i>0, requires\n\n    <z_0,w_i> >= <P_i,w_i>+sigma_i T>0.\n\nSumming over all N original queries, terminal included, gives -N>0\nbecause sum_i w_i=(0,N/R), a contradiction. Therefore the only\npotentially legal geometric domain is\n\n    0<R^(-2)<M.                                       (2.6)\n\nInside it, T<2M<2001/1000. The final bound follows from\nM=1+zeta/N^2 and the declared range of N.\n\n## 3. Mechanism I: exact terminal budget from positive original-row weights\n\nSuppose the entire prescribed itinerary is legal. Set\n\n    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t v_t,\n    A_i=||v_i||^2/2+<P_i,v_i>,\n    H=sum_(i=0)^n 1/sigma_i,\n    B_t=sum_(i=0)^t 1/sigma_i.\n\nHere H is a positive scalar weight sum, not the physical level H_l.\nThe actual function formula gives <z_0,v_i>=f_i+A_i. Divide by\nsigma_i and sum over ALL original vertices:\n\n    sum_i (f_i+A_i)/sigma_i=-N.                        (3.1)\n\nThis uses the INITIAL vertical slice, not a false assumption that the\nvertical coordinate stays fixed later. In fact it moves by\n-h_i sigma_i/R at every physical step.\n\nLet Delta_t=f_t-f_(t+1). The two adjacent original rows give exactly\n\n    L_t=h_t<v_t,v_(t+1)>+||v_t-v_(t+1)||^2/2\n          <=Delta_t\n          <=h_t||v_t||^2-||v_t-v_(t+1)||^2/2=U_t.      (3.2)\n\nSubstitute f_i=f_n+sum_(t=i)^(n-1)Delta_t into (3.1). Since every\nB_t is positive,\n\n    f_n=[-N-sum_i A_i/sigma_i-sum_(t<n)B_t Delta_t]/H\n        <=[-N-Z]/H=:Fmax,\n    Z=sum_i A_i/sigma_i+sum_(t<n)B_t L_t.              (3.3)\n\nThe original terminal zero-vertex row also requires\nf_n>=||v_n||^2/2>0. Consequently a proved Z>=0 excludes EVERY\ninitialization for the fixed itinerary. Choosing lower decrements\nmaximizes the formal terminal value under the weighted slice relation;\nit does not by itself prove the existence of a function or trajectory.\n\n## 4. Evaluation of the budget: a general explicit all-m criterion\n\nThe following bound evaluates Z, using all original steps and two\nparticular positive contributions, rather than leaving it as a sum.\nSuppose known constants 0<q<=b bound every prescribed radius:\n\n    q<=sigma_i<=b, 0<=i<=n.\n\nWrite v=R^(-2), gamma=M-v>=0. Every frame Gram is at least -gamma\nby Cauchy-Schwarz on the horizontal coordinates. Expand the first\nterm of Z:\n\n    sum_i A_i/sigma_i\n       =(T/2)sum_i sigma_i\n          +sum_(t<n)h_t sigma_t sum_(i>t)<w_t,w_i>\n       >=-b gamma N S.                               (4.1)\n\nFor all t, the squared-distance term in L_t is nonnegative, so\nL_t>=-h_t b^2 gamma. Also B_t<=N/q. Thus every omitted step in the\nnext lower bound costs at most gamma N b^2 h_t/q.\n\nNow KEEP the TWO original level-(m-2) steps, whose time indices are\nN/4-1 and 3N/4-1. Direct substitution into E_(m-2) gives the exact\nidentity\n\n    T-2E_(m-2)\n      =v+1/(N-1)+zeta(m-3)/(N^2 Kmix)>v.               (4.2)\n\nEach of these two L_t is at least H_(m-2) q^2 v, and\nB_t>=(t+1)/b. Their combined positive contribution is therefore at\nleast N H_(m-2) q^2 v/b. Charging every other contribution, even\novercharging by using the whole S for the negative part, yields\n\n    sum_(t<n)B_t L_t\n       >=N H_(m-2) q^2 v/b - gamma N S b^2/q.          (4.3)\n\nTo compare the bridge with the FULL word mass, exact level counting\ngives\n\n    S=[a/(c-2)]c^m+[1-a/(c-2)]N-1<c^m.\n\nHere 0<a/(c-2)<1 because c>1+sqrt(2) and\n(1+sqrt(2))((1+sqrt(2))-2)=1. Also\nH_(m-2)>c^(m-2)/3, hence H_(m-2)>S/20 from c<5/2.\nCombining (4.1)-(4.3) proves the EVALUATED bound\n\n    Z >= N S [q^2 v/(20b) - gamma(b+b^2/q)].           (4.4)\n\nIn particular define the explicit positive constant\n\n    kappa(q,b)=q^3/[20b^2(q+b)].\n\nFor any radii with the stated bounds, the whole prescribed trajectory\nis impossible whenever\n\n    M/(1+kappa(q,b)) <= R^(-2) < M.                   (4.5)\n\nIndeed (4.5) makes the bracket in (4.4) nonnegative, so\nFmax<=-N/H<=-q<0, contradicting (2.5). Equality at the lower boundary\nis also excluded. This is a nonempty band of the previously surviving\nnonacute region. It is not an upper bound on the full W_n(h).\n\nAll time indices, the terminal term in (3.1), and every child mass\nare original. No transition has been chronologically reordered or\ndeleted. Equations (4.1) and (4.3) lower-bound, rather than ignore,\nevery contribution not selected as a positive one.\n\n## 5. Explicit radius bounds and resulting rejected parameter regions\n\nWe now supply q,b analytically for the actual law (1.1).\nFor a short step H_l=1+d, its root identity is\n\n    (1-rho_l)(rho_l+d)=2(1-d)E_l rho_l/T.\n\nCounting (1.1)'s weights gives E_0<2/N and E_1<4/N, hence\n1-rho_0<=4/N and 1-rho_1<=8eps/N. The elementary inequality\n\n    -x/(1-x)<=log(1-x)<=-x, 0<=x<1,\n\nfollows by integrating 1/(1-u). It gives\nlog rho_0>=-5/N and log rho_1>=-9eps/N. The exact full-word counts\nat levels 0 and 1 are N/2 and N/4. Thus their undamped-root product\nis greater than exp(-3). The EXTRA damping factor is exactly\n\n    (1-delta_0/N)^(N/2)(1-delta_1/N)^(N/4)\n      >=exp[-(delta_0/2+delta_1/4)/(1-16/N)]>exp(-13).\n\nEvery higher ratio is at least 1, and no prefix has more short-level\ncounts than the whole word. Therefore\n\n    sigma_i>exp(-16) for EVERY original i.             (5.1)\n\nThis jointly derives, rather than assumes, the previous radius bound.\nIt also shows why O(N) occurrences of O(1/N) damping are not an\nexponential-loss argument in N or m.\n\nFor a high step H_l>2, substitution of H_l-1 into its root polynomial\ngives (H_l-1)(H_l-2)(1+t_l)>0. Hence rho_l<H_l-1, so\nlog r_l<=theta(rho_l-1)<theta H_l. Dropping negative short logs and\ncounting every high occurrence gives\n\n    log sigma_i<=theta S.\n\nThus the explicit all-m restriction\n\n    0<=theta<=1/(16S)                                 (5.2)\n\nimplies sigma_i<exp(1/16)<=16/15<9/8. Taking\n\n    q=exp(-16), b=9/8,\n    kappa_*=exp(-48)/[20(9/8)^2(exp(-16)+9/8)]\n\nin (4.5) proves a nonempty, completely explicit excluded band for\nALL 0<delta_0,delta_1<=16:\n\n    theta<=1/(16S),\n    M/(1+kappa_*)<=R^(-2)<M.                          (5.3)\n\nFor this entire all-m region, Fmax<=-exp(-16). The band is narrow,\nbut its width is an explicit positive constant fraction of M and is\nindependent of m. No claim that it covers general theta=O(1/m) is made.\n\nA wider and simpler example follows when both deltas are at most 1/8\nand R^(-2)>=M/2. In this case T>=3/2. Since a>1/3,\n2(1-a)/(1+a)<1 and the root identity improves to\n1-rho_0<=E_0/T. More explicitly,\n\n    E_0<=1/N+3/N^2<=17/(16N),\n    log rho_0>=-3/(4N), log rho_1>=-6eps/N.\n\nThe first log bound follows from\n(17/24)/(1-17/(24N))<=3/4, and the second from the short-root\nidentity and E_1<4/N. The total damping log is greater than -1/10,\nas delta_0/2+delta_1/4<=3/32. Thus every prefix radius exceeds\nexp(-3/8-(3/2)eps-1/10)>exp(-1/2)>1/2.\nThe last inequality follows, for example, from exp(x)<1/(1-x)\nat x=1/2. Under (5.2) we may consequently use q=1/2,b=9/8 in (4.4):\n\n    Z >= NS [R^(-2)/90 -(117/32)(M-R^(-2))].           (5.4)\n\nThis excludes\n\n    0<delta_0,delta_1<=1/8, theta<=1/(16S),\n    (5265/5281)M<=R^(-2)<M.                           (5.5)\n\nFor the explicit interior subband R^(-2)>=999M/1000, the bracket\nin (5.4) exceeds M/200, since\n999/90000-117/32000=1191/160000>1/200. As H<2N,\n\n    Fmax< -1/2-SM/400.                                (5.6)\n\nThese are actual all-m sign and magnitude estimates for the original\nterminal budget, not an unevaluated recurrence. Section 7 will exclude\nthe small-delta region even more broadly by a different mechanism.\n\n## 6. Mechanism II: explicit lower-decrement/Walsh initialization\n\nFor the fixed word and vertices, set\n\n    C_i=sum_(t<i)L_t,\n    f_0=[-N-sum_i(A_i-C_i)/sigma_i]/H,\n    f_i=f_0-C_i, F_i=f_i+A_i,\n    q_i=F_i/sigma_i,\n    x_(0,t)=(1/(N b_t))sum_i chi_t(Gamma(i))q_i\n       for EVERY nonzero character coordinate t,\n    z_0=(x_0,-R), z_i=z_0-P_i.                        (6.1)\n\nAll sums are explicit finite definitions from the prescribed word and\nparameters. No optimization or numerical fitting is required to state\nthis candidate, and no such computation was executed. Since sum_i q_i=-N,\nWalsh orthogonality gives\n\n    <x_0,U_(Gamma(j))>=q_j+1,\n    <z_0,v_j>=F_j,\n    <z_j,v_j>-||v_j||^2/2=f_j.                        (6.2)\n\nThus the reconstruction of the formal values is exact in the required\ninitial slice. They are ACTUAL values of F_D only if ALL projection\nnormals hold. In particular negative formal values do not mean a\nnegative value of the nonnegative function F_D.\n\nFor this candidate every adjacent lower decrement is saturated,\nDelta_t=L_t. Equivalently Q_(t+1,t)=0, the original residual at query\nt being perpendicular to v_(t+1)-v_t. The opposite adjacent row is\nnonpositive by the local firm inequality. No remote or zero row\nfollows automatically.\n\nIn the band (5.3), this explicit candidate has f_n=Fmax<=-exp(-16),\nso its ORIGINAL terminal zero-vertex normal is\n\n    <z_n-v_n,-v_n>=||v_n||^2/2-f_n\n       >=||v_n||^2/2+exp(-16)>0.                      (6.3)\n\nUnder (5.6) it is larger than ||v_n||^2/2+1/2+SM/400.\nThese are precisely identified failed original rows. They cannot be\nrepaired by relabeling the formal f_i as legal function values.\n\nWe next derive a separate REMOTE row obstruction that applies well\noutside this near-acute budget band and extends to every value choice.\n\n## 7. A positive original cycle with strict damping\n\nConsider ORIGINAL queries 1,2,3, whose two physical steps are\nH_1=1+beta followed by H_0=1+a. Let\n\n    s=sigma_1>0, u=r_1, v=r_0,\n    e=E_1, f=E_0, J as in (2.1),\n    p_0=v_1=sW_0, p_1=v_2=suW_1, p_2=v_3=suvW_2.\n\nTheir successive Gray flips are 1 and 0, with exact unscaled Gram\nentries T-2e, T-2f and T-2e-2f+4J. The strict damping is retained.\nDefine the two nonnegative adjacent firm gaps\n\n    k_0=-<(beta p_0+p_1),p_1-p_0>,\n    k_1=-<(a p_1+p_2),p_2-p_1>.\n\nAlso define\n\n    B=<a p_1+p_2,p_1-p_0>\n      =s^2 u[(a+v)A+2vf(1-u)-4vJ],\n    A=T(u-1)+2e.                                     (7.1)\n\nThe expansion in (7.1) uses the three full Euclidean Gram entries,\nincluding the moving vertical coordinate; no root equality is used.\n\nAdd the three original ordered interpolation rows for the cycle\n2 -> 1, 3 -> 2, 1 -> 3, in the Q convention (2.4). All function\nvalues cancel. Direct substitution of the two physical steps yields\n\n    Q_(2,1)+Q_(3,2)+Q_(1,3)=B-k_0-k_1=:C.             (7.2)\n\nFor an explicit check, write local d_0=beta, d_1=a. The right side\nexpands to\n\n    -d_0||p_0||^2+(d_0-1-d_1)<p_0,p_1>\n      +||p_1||^2+d_1<p_1,p_2>-<p_0,p_2>+||p_2||^2,\n\nwhich is exactly the sum of the three rows after the two GD updates.\nEvery legal interpolation row must be nonpositive. Hence C>0 is a\nvalue-independent obstruction to EVERY initial point. For the\nspecific candidate (6.1), its first two rows in (7.2) are zero, so\nits ORIGINAL query-3 normal against vertex v_1 is exactly\n\n    <z_3-v_3,v_1-v_3>=Q_(1,3)=C.                      (7.3)\n\nThis is not the old double-root argument: both k_0 and k_1 are\nstrictly positive and are paid explicitly.\n\n## 8. Uniform evaluation of the cycle and the rejected damping wedge\n\nBy Section 2, the acute domain is already impossible for the full\nitinerary. Work now in R^(-2)<M, so T<2001/1000. From the short-root\nbounds and delta_l<=16,\n\n    u>=1-17/N, v>=1-20/N,\n    u,v>=999/1000,\n    J<=1/N+(5/2)/N^2<=1001/(1000N).                   (8.1)\n\nFor clarity, Kmix>=N/2 in the present m range. Thus the mixed part\nof J is at most 1/(2N^2), while\n1/(N-1)<=1/N+2/N^2, proving its displayed bound. The bounds on u,v\nuse 1-rho_0<=4/N, 1-rho_1<=8eps/N and eps<1/1000.\nN>=32768 is already enough for (8.1); the actual range is much larger.\n\nLet u_bar=rho_1 before damping. Its exact root identity gives\n\n    T(u_bar-1)+2e=2e beta(1+u_bar)/(u_bar+beta)>2e beta.\n\nSince u=u_bar(1-delta_1/N), this implies\n\n    A>2e beta-T delta_1/N\n      >[5994/1000-(2001/1000)delta_1]/N.               (8.2)\n\nSuppose\n\n    401 delta_0+681 delta_1<437.                       (8.3)\n\nThen delta_1<1, so the lower bound in (8.2) is positive. Also\na+v>1399/1000. Discard the positive term 2vf(1-u) in (7.1),\nuse v<=1, and then u>=999/1000. The bracket stays positive for\ndelta_1<1, giving the convenient rational lower bound\n\n    B > (s^2/N)[437/100-(14/5)delta_1].                (8.4)\n\nAll constants in this simplification can be checked exactly: before\nrounding outward, the constant coefficient is\n4377224394/1000000000>437/100 and the magnitude of the delta_1\ncoefficient is 2796599601/1000000000<14/5. These rational inequalities\ncome from multiplying the displayed symbolic bounds; they are not a\nnumerical trajectory or an external arithmetic computation.\n\nWe must also PAY both firm gaps. Let P_d(r)=r^2+(d-1)t r-d for the\nappropriate low-level cosine t>0 and root rho. Since\nr=rho(1-delta/N),\n\n    -P_d(r)=(rho-r)[rho+r+(d-1)t]<=2delta/N.\n\nBoth cosines are positive because E_1<4/N and T>1. Therefore\n\n    k_0<=2s^2 T delta_1/N,\n    k_1<=2s^2 u^2 T delta_0/N,\n    k_0+k_1<(s^2/N)(401/100)(delta_0+delta_1).          (8.5)\n\nCombining (8.4)-(8.5) proves the EVALUATED positive-cycle bound\n\n    C>(s^2/(100N))[437-401delta_0-681delta_1]>0.         (8.6)\n\nThus (8.3) excludes the whole prescribed radial itinerary, for every\nm>=1536, every zeta in (0,1], every theta in [0,1], and EVERY R>0:\nSection 2 handles R^(-2)>=M, while (8.6) handles the rest. No\ninitialization, no choice of actual function values, and no adjustment\nof any later large-step ratio repairs this three-query cycle.\n\nIn particular the nonempty rectangle\n\n    0<delta_0,delta_1<=2/5                            (8.7)\n\nis excluded uniformly. Indeed its worst right-side bracket is\n437-(401+681)(2/5)=21/5, so\n\n    C>21s^2/(500N)>s^2/(25N).                         (8.8)\n\nFor the explicit lower-decrement/Walsh candidate this is exactly the\nstrictly positive remote normal (7.3). For arbitrary values, (7.2)\nis a sum of three original rows which would all have to be\nnonpositive. The obstruction therefore strengthens a candidate-level\nfailure into a whole-itinerary failure on the stated parameter scope.\nNo claim is made at or beyond the boundary of (8.3) from this estimate.\n\n## 9. True-radius accounting and precise remaining domain\n\nFor any hypothetical legal prescribed trajectory from z_0=(x_0,-R),\nthe actual vertical coordinate is exactly\n\n    (z_i)_vertical=-R-R^(-1)sum_(t<i)h_t sigma_t.\n\nIts true initial price is\n\n    R_init=dist((x_0,-R),C^polar),\n\nnot automatically ||x_0||. The latter is only the distance to the\nparticular valid minimum z_star=(0,-R). Positive origin-normal cap\nheight <z_i-v_i,v_i> and slack about another polar minimum remain\ndistinct quantities. The score, only if all original normals hold,\nwould be\n\n    [2<(x_0,-R),v_n>-2 sum_(t<n)h_t<v_t,v_n>-||v_n||^2]\n       /R_init^2.\n\nNo paid score is attached to a candidate that fails (6.3) or (7.3).\nIn those regions the construction is invalid before any closest-radius\noptimization.
