Thus this exclusion includes\n   genuinely order-1/m theta, rather than only a theta*S small\n   perturbation. It again retains the complete damping rectangle.\n   The same original budget gives f_n<=-exp(-16)-(T/2)exp(-32).\n4. As a shared elementary screen, R^(-2)>=M is impossible for every\n   positive choice of radii. The remaining nonacute parameter region is\n   explicitly stated in Section 10. In particular the lower half of that\n   region has not been excluded or turned into a legal witness.\n\nAll these assertions are joint draft mathematics, not verifier-accepted\nfacts. Restricted-family infeasibility is not a full-function upper\ncertificate and does not resolve the original minimax question.\n\n## 1. Fixed original model and full parameter geometry\n\nThe original target uses\n\n    W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),\n    W_n^star=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h), W=2R,\n    U_n^ZJ=1/(2S_bullet(n)+1).\n\nIt asks whether W_n^star/U_n^ZJ tends to 1 for EVERY sufficiently large\nhorizon and EVERY predetermined nonnegative word, with a threshold\nindependent of that word. No change to that quantifier or normalization\nis made. No direction of the accepted upper comparison B_n/D_m<47/50\nis used or reversed here.\n\nKeep the SAME enclosing integer m>=1536 throughout, including subblocks:\n\n    N=2^m, n=N-1, eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,\n    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    H_l=1+a c^l, h_i=H_(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n,\n    S=sum_(i<n)h_i.\n\nThe fixed pre-run parameters are\n\n    R>0, 0<zeta<=1, 0<delta_0,delta_1<=16, 0<=theta<=1.\n\nThe bounds beta>999/1000, 2<c<5/2, 1/3<a<1/2 follow, for example,\nfrom eps<1/1000 and 1+sqrt(2)<17/7. Thus H_0,H_1<2 and H_l>2\nfor l>=2. Exact level counts give\n\n    S=[a/(c-2)]c^m+[1-a/(c-2)]N-1 < c^m.             (1.1)\n\nIndeed 0<a/(c-2)<1: if lambda=1+sqrt(2), then\nlambda(lambda-2)=1, while c>lambda and beta<1.\nThe displayed expression is a strict convex combination of c^m and N,\nminus 1. In particular\n\n    S < (15/2) H_(m-1).                               (1.2)\n\nFor each k=0,...,m-1 let tau_k=N-2^k. Define\n\n    Kmix=N-1-m,\n    eta=zeta/(N^2 Kmix),\n    b_(tau_k)^2=2^k/(N-1),\n    b_t^2=eta for every other nonzero character t,\n    U_s(t)=b_t (-1)^(t dot s),\n    M=1+eta Kmix=1+zeta/N^2,\n    v=R^(-2), T=M+v,\n    w_s=(U_s,1/R), Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2).\n\nEvery character weight remains strictly positive. Write w_i for\nw_(Gamma(i)) only when i denotes an ORIGINAL time index. The actual\nfixed vertices and one static convex body are\n\n    v_i=sigma_i w_i,\n    D=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n},\n    F_D(z)=max_(g in D) [<z,g>-||g||^2/2].\n\nPhysical level-l flips have exact energy and Gram value\n\n    E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta(N/2-l-1),\n    G_l=T-2E_l\n       =v+(N+1-2^(l+2))/(N-1)+eta(2l+1-m).          (1.3)\n\nThe positive root rho_l is given by\n\n    rho_l^2+(H_l-2)(G_l/T)rho_l+1-H_l=0,\n\n    rho_l=[-(H_l-2)(G_l/T)\n       +sqrt((H_l-2)^2(G_l/T)^2+4(H_l-1))]/2,\n\nand the prescribed law remains exactly\n\n    r_l=rho_l(1-delta_l/N), l=0,1,\n    r_l=1+theta(rho_l-1), 2<=l<m,\n    sigma_0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i r_(nu_2(i+1)).       (1.4)\n\nThe polynomial at 1 is (2-H_l)(1-G_l/T). Therefore the low rho_l\nare in (0,1) and the high rho_l exceed 1. All radii are positive.\nLocal firm inequalities hold but are not used as global feasibility.\n\n## 2. Static function, original normals, and terminal budget\n\nWalsh orthogonality follows by pairing binary labels differing in a bit.\nThe full Walsh matrix, including its constant row, is invertible.\nConsequently the N frames w_s and their positive radial rescalings are\nlinearly independent. D is a full-dimensional simplex in R^N.\n\nCompleting the square identifies the unique maximizer in F_D with\nP_D(z). Projection is characterized exactly by\n\n    g in D and <z-g,w-g><=0 for every vertex w of D.    (2.1)\n\nExpanding squared distance proves sufficiency; differentiation along\nsegments proves necessity. Adding two normal inequalities proves firm\nnonexpansiveness of P_D. Comparing the two maximizing affine functions\nthen proves that F_D is differentiable with gradient P_D, which is\n1-Lipschitz. F_D is finite and convex. In the interior of D it equals\n||z||^2/2, so its least gradient Lipschitz constant is exactly 1.\n\nWith C=cone{v_i}, the FULL minimum is\n\n    min F_D=0,\n    argmin F_D=C^polar\n      ={(x,y): <x,U_s>+y/R<=0 for EVERY s}.             (2.2)\n\nC is closed as an invertible linear image of the nonnegative orthant.\nEvery polar point has value zero; a positive dot product with a generator\ngives positive value on a short segment toward that generator. This also\nproves the converse in (2.2). In particular (0,-R) is a polar minimizer.\n\nSuppose, only for deriving necessary conditions, that a physical run from\nz_0=(x_0,-R) has all the prescribed v_i as its actual gradients. Then\n\n    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t v_t, z_i=z_0-P_i,\n    f_i=<z_i,v_i>-||v_i||^2/2.\n\nEvery original ordered interpolation row is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<v_i,z_j-z_i>+||v_i-v_j||^2/2\n        =<z_j-v_j,v_i-v_j><=0.                        (2.3)\n\nThe zero-vertex normal requires\n\n    f_i>=||v_i||^2/2.                                 (2.4)\n\nPut\n\n    A_i=||v_i||^2/2+<P_i,v_i>,\n    B_t=sum_(i=0)^t 1/sigma_i,\n    H=sum_(i=0)^n 1/sigma_i.\n\nBecause sum_i w_i=(0,N/R), summing <z_0,v_i>=f_i+A_i after\ndivision by sigma_i gives the exact INITIAL-slice relation\n\n    sum_i(f_i+A_i)/sigma_i=-N.                        (2.5)\n\nThe actual vertical coordinate subsequently changes; no fixed-slice\nassumption is made about z_i for i>0. The two adjacent original rows\ngive, with Delta_t=f_t-f_(t+1),\n\n    L_t=h_t<v_t,v_(t+1)>+||v_t-v_(t+1)||^2/2\n          <=Delta_t\n          <=h_t||v_t||^2-||v_t-v_(t+1)||^2/2.          (2.6)\n\nExpanding f_i=f_n+sum_(t=i)^(n-1)Delta_t in (2.5), using B_t>0,\nyields\n\n    Z=sum_i A_i/sigma_i+sum_(t<n)B_t L_t,\n    f_n<=(-N-Z)/H=:Fmax.                              (2.7)\n\nIn particular Z>=0 contradicts the strictly positive terminal lower\nbound (2.4). This obstruction concerns every choice of initialization\nand function values compatible with this prescribed itinerary.\n\nIf v>=M then every frame Gram is nonnegative. The original zero-vertex\nrow gives <z_0,w_i>>=sigma_i T+<P_i,w_i>>0. Summing all N such\nrows contradicts sum_i<z_0,w_i>=-N. Thus v>=M is impossible for\narbitrary positive radii. The substantive new work below concerns v<M.\n\n## 3. Exact binary factors and exact summation of all mixed characters\n\nFor an original index i, the number of level-l transitions before i is\n\n    c_l(i)=floor(i/2^l)-floor(i/2^(l+1))\n          =floor(i/2^(l+1))+bit_l(i).\n\nExpanding the floor in the higher binary digits proves, without any\ntrajectory replay,\n\n    Q_k=r_k product_(l<k) r_l^(2^(k-l-1)),\n    sigma_i=product_(k<m) Q_k^bit_k(i).                (3.1)\n\nFor every integer exponent b, including negative b, and every binary\ncharacter xi, direct distributivity therefore gives\n\n    sum_(i<N) sigma_i^b (-1)^(xi dot bits(i))\n      =product_(k<m)[1+(-1)^xi_k Q_k^b].              (3.2)\n\nAll factors are positive before the character sign, so negative powers\nare well defined. Equation (3.2) is an exact finite identity.\n\nGray bit l equals i_l xor i_(l+1), with i_m=0. Xoring from k to m-1\ntelescopes, proving\n\n    chi_(tau_k)(Gamma(i))=(-1)^bit_k(i).               (3.3)\n\nThe Gray map is invertible and linear over binary xor. Consequently its\nremaining nonzero characters are precisely the binary characters other\nthan the m singletons. Summing their COMPLEMENT by Walsh orthogonality\ngives the exact full Gram kernel\n\n    <w_i,w_j>=v-eta\n       +sum_(k<m)[2^k/(N-1)-eta](-1)^(i_k+j_k)\n       +eta N 1_(i=j).                               (3.4)\n\nIn particular the tiny terms are neither deleted nor replaced by an\nunspecified perturbation. The diagonal in (3.4) is exactly T.\n\nThere is a useful exact original-tail identity. Partition i>t by the\nhighest bit k at which i and t differ. Such a block occurs exactly when\nt_k=0, has 2^k elements, has equal higher bits, opposite bit k, and\nuniform lower bits. The mean of (3.4) on this block is\n\n    V_k=v+(N-3*2^k)/(N-1)-eta(m-k-1).\n\nHence\n\n    C_t:=sum_(i>t)<w_t,w_i>\n       =sum_(k:bit_k(t)=0) 2^k V_k.                  (3.5)\n\nThis partitions the actual chronological suffix; it does not reorder\nthe physical word.\n\nFor later use, the worst V_k is the top one:\n\n    V_(m-1)=v-N/[2(N-1)],\n    V_k>0 for k<=m-2 whenever v>=M/2.                 (3.6)\n\nIndeed 2^k<=N/4 in the latter range, and\nV_k>=v+N/[4(N-1)]-eta m>0. Here Kmix>=m and eta m<=1/N^2.\nThus all C_t are nonnegative if v>=N/[2(N-1)]. If only v>=M/2,\nthe sole possibly negative summand in (3.5) is the top one, giving\n\n    C_t >= -N/[4(N-1)] >= -1/2.                      (3.7)\n\nThe small distinction between M/2 and N/[2(N-1)] is retained.\n\n## 4. Closed finite product formula for the entire budget\n\nThe following exact formula applies to the whole law, including ALL\n0<delta_0,delta_1<=16, all theta in [0,1], and all R>0. It is not\nrestricted to the sign regions proved later.\n\nDefine empty products as 1 and put\n\n    P_l=product_(j<l)Q_j,\n    D_l=product_(j<l)(1+Q_j),\n    J_l=product_(j<l)(1+Q_j^(-1)),\n    F_l=product_(j>l)(1+Q_j).\n\nFor every transition of level l, the lower bits of t are 1, bit l is\n0, and the higher bits are free. By (3.1) and (3.5),\n\n    X_l:=sum_(t:nu_2(t+1)=l) sigma_t C_t\n       =P_l F_l [2^l V_l\n                    +sum_(k>l) 2^k V_k/(1+Q_k)].     (4.1)\n\nThe positive step-weight sum is also a closed finite product expression:\n\n    W_l:=sum_(t:nu_2(t+1)=l) B_t sigma_t^2\n       =P_l D_l F_l\n        +P_l^2 sum_(k>l) Q_k^2 J_k F_k\n                         product_(l<j<k)(1+Q_j^2).   (4.2)\n\nTo derive (4.2), expand B_t sigma_t^2=sum_(i<=t)sigma_t^2/sigma_i.\nThe term i=t and terms whose highest differing bit is k<l sum to\nP_l D_l F_l, using\n1+sum_(k<l)Q_k product_(j<k)(1+Q_j)=D_l.\nFor highest differing bit k>l, t_k=1 and i_k=0. Higher common bits\ngive F_k, free lower i bits give J_k, fixed lower t bits give P_l^2,\nand its free bits between l and k give product(1+Q_j^2). This is\nexactly the second term. Highest differing bit l is impossible.\n\nSet\n\n    ell_l=(T/2)(1+r_l^2)+(H_l-1)r_l G_l\n         =H_l r_l G_l+(T/2)(1-r_l)^2+2r_l E_l.\n\nThen L_t=sigma_t^2 ell_l at level l. The whole terminal budget and\nits denominator are therefore\n\n    Z=(T/2)D_m+sum_(l<m) H_l X_l+sum_(l<m)ell_l W_l,\n    H=J_m.                                           (4.3)\n\nHere D_m in (4.3) means the local product product_(j<m)(1+Q_j),\nNOT the unrelated word scalar 1+2S. Equation (4.3), together with\nthe explicit roots in (1.4), contains no evolving recurrence and at\nmost double sums over levels. It retains all physical steps, original\nterminal rows, radial weights, and mixed characters. The proofs below\nevaluate its sign over full infinite parameter domains; they do not\nmerely display it as a proposed future computation.\n\n## 5. Uniform lower radius bound, jointly derived\n\nEvery prescribed radius, for any theta in [0,1], obeys\n\n    sigma_i>q_0:=exp(-16).                            (5.1)\n\nHere is a direct derivation. Counting (1.3)'s weights gives E_0<2/N\nand E_1<4/N. For a short step H_l=1+d, the root equation rearranges as\n\n    (1-rho_l)(rho_l+d)=2(1-d)E_l rho_l/T.\n\nSince T>1, this implies 1-rho_0<=4/N and\n1-rho_1<=8eps/N. Integration of 1/(1-u) gives\n-x/(1-x)<=log(1-x)<=-x, and consequently\n\n    log rho_0>=-5/N, log rho_1>=-9eps/N.\n\nThe maximum prefix counts of those two levels are N/2 and N/4.\nTheir undamped contribution is thus greater than exp(-3). The added\ndamping contribution at every prefix is at least\n\n    exp[-(delta_0/2+delta_1/4)/(1-16/N)]>exp(-13).\n\nEvery high ratio is at least 1. Combining these facts proves (5.1),\nuniformly even when the two deltas depend explicitly on m.\n\nIn particular H<=N/q_0. At theta=0 all ratios are at most 1, so in\nthat case also sigma_i<=1. No claim of exponential decay in m or N\nis inferred from the many short steps.\n\n## 6. Mechanism I: exact theta=0 bridge weights\n\nSpecialize theta=0 but retain the ENTIRE damping rectangle. Write\n\n    x=r_0, y=r_1, p=x^2 y, 0<x,y,p<1,\n    d=(1+x)(1+xy)=1+x+xy+p,\n    K=d/(1-p)>0.\n\nEquation (3.1), or the exact level counts, gives\n\n    sigma_(4q+b)=p^q s_b, (s_0,s_1,s_2,s_3)=(1,x,xy,p),\n    Q_0=x, Q_1=xy, Q_k=p^(2^(k-2)) for k>=2.          (6.1)\n\nFor EVERY original high-level bridge, t=4q+3. Summing all preceding\ninverse radii block by block is a finite geometric sum and yields\n\n    B_t sigma_t=K(1-sigma_t),\n    W(t):=B_t sigma_t^2=K sigma_t(1-sigma_t),\n    sigma_t=p^((t+1)/4).                              (6.2)\n\nFor example the inverse-radius mass of one four-query block is\n1+1/x+1/(xy)+1/p=d/p. Multiplying its geometric prefix sum by\nsigma_t=p^(q+1) proves (6.2) exactly. No limiting replacement is used.\n\nWe require a uniform dyadic comparison. For every 0<z<1,\n\n    [z(1-z)+z^3(1-z^3)]/[z^2(1-z^2)] > 3/2.         (6.3)\n\nIndeed the ratio is [z^(-1)+z+z^2+z^3]/(1+z). Using\nz^(-1)+z>=2, the numerator after subtracting 3(1+z)/2 is at least\n\n    1/2-3z/2+z^2+z^3\n      =(z+2)(z-1/2)^2+z/4>0.\n\nThe same comparison holds for translated dyadic parent/child bridges.\nFor a parent with sigma=w=z^b, b>=2, its two child values are w/z\nand wz. Their weight ratio is\n\n    [A-w(A^2-2)]/(1-w), A=z+z^(-1)>=2.\n\nThis expression is nonincreasing in w, since its derivative is\n-(A-2)(A+1)/(1-w)^2<=0. As w<=z^2, its minimum for these parents\nis at w=z^2, exactly (6.3). This argument keeps the TRUE global B_t;\nit does not restart the cumulative weight in a translated child.\n\nLet W_top=W_(m-1), the single weight at t=N/2-1. Summing the child\ncomparisons over the two highest generations gives\n\n    W_(m-2)>(3/2)W_top,\n    W_(m-3)>(9/4)W_top.                               (6.4)\n\nThis is the binary-budget gain which was absent from a worst-cross-Gram\nestimate. It is independent of the total size of the two damping terms.\n\n## 7. Mechanism I evaluated: the upper half of the nonacute domain\n\nAssume theta=0 and M/2<=v<M. From (1.3), exactly,\n\n    G_(m-1)=v-1+eta(m-1) >= v-1,\n    G_(m-2)=v+1/(N-1)+eta(m-3) > v,\n    G_(m-3)=v+1/2+3/[2(N-1)]+eta(m-5) > v+1/2.\n\nEvery other G_l is positive because\nE_(l+1)-E_l=2^(l+1)/(N-1)-eta>0.\nThe squared-distance remainder in ell_l is nonnegative. Thus retain\nonly the top three levels, using r_l=1 at those levels and safely\ndiscarding the other nonnegative B_t L_t contributions. Equations\n(6.4), H_(m-2)>H_(m-1)/c, H_(m-3)>H_(m-1)/c^2, and c<5/2 give\n\n    sum_(t<n)B_t L_t\n      >H_(m-1)W_top [(v-1)+(3/5)v+(9/25)(v+1/2)]\n      =H_(m-1)W_top [(49/25)v-41/50]\n      >=(4/25)H_(m-1)W_top.                           (7.1)\n\nThe last inequality uses v>=M/2>=1/2. The possibly negative central\nbridge is explicitly paid; it is not discarded.\n\nThere are N/2 terms in B_(N/2-1), each at least 1, while (5.1) gives\nsigma_(N/2-1)>q_0. Consequently\n\n    W_top>(N/2)q_0^2,\n    sum B_t L_t>(2/25)N H_(m-1)exp(-32).              (7.2)\n\nEquation (3.7) and sigma_t<=1 handle the ENTIRE other part of Z:\n\n    sum_i A_i/sigma_i\n      =(T/2)sum_i sigma_i+sum_(t<n)h_t sigma_t C_t\n      >=(T/2)sum_i sigma_i-S/2.                       (7.3)\n\nUsing (1.2), (7.2), and (7.3),\n\n    Z>(T/2)sum_i sigma_i\n         +H_(m-1)[(2/25)N exp(-32)-15/4]\n      >N H_(m-1)exp(-32)/25>0.                       (7.4)\n\nThe last comparison is uniform in m. For example e<4 implies\nN exp(-32)>2^(m-64)>100, and 100>375/4. These elementary estimates\nare analytic inequalities, not an arithmetic or numerical replay.\n\nSince H<=N exp(16), (2.7) gives\n\n    Fmax<-exp(-16)-H_(m-1)exp(-48)/25<0.              (7.5)\n\nThis contradicts the ORIGINAL terminal zero-vertex row\nf_n>=T sigma_n^2/2>0. The entire prescribed law is therefore excluded\nfor theta=0 and M/2<=v<M, uniformly over ALL allowed deltas and zeta.\nTogether with the acute screen, theta=0 is excluded whenever v>=M/2.\nNo conclusion of feasibility or infeasibility is asserted here for\n0<v<M/2 at theta=0.\n\n## 8. Mechanism II: the true later-child budget pays a large center jump\n\nThis mechanism extends the evaluated binary budget into genuinely\norder-1/m theta. It uses the original law and dictionary unchanged.\nIts new ingredient is the quadratic weight of the child AFTER the\ncenter jump, rather than a small perturbation of theta=0.\n\nAssume\n\n    v>=v_c:=N/[2(N-1)], v<M.                          (8.1)\n\nThen v>=M/2, since zeta/N^2<1/(N-1). All V_k, hence all C_t, are\nnonnegative by (3.6). Thus the first part of Z is at least\n(T/2)sum_i sigma_i, without needing an upper bound on any radius.\nEvery G_l with l<=m-2 is positive. Only the center may have negative\nG_(m-1). (8.2)\n\nThe true inverse-radius prefix satisfies B_(3d-1)>B_(2d-1), so\n\n    W(3d-1)/W(2d-1)>(Q/q)^2,\n    r_- W(3d-1)>r_+ Q W(2d-1).                       (8.3)\n\nThere is no reset of B, no deletion of the right child, and no\nassumption that its radii remain bounded above.\n\nSince G_(m-2)>v and H_(m-2)>H_(m-1)/c, (8.3) gives\n\n    B_(3d-1)L_(3d-1)\n      >[H_(m-1)/c] v r_+ Q W(2d-1),\n    B_(2d-1)L_(2d-1)\n      >=-H_(m-1)g r_+ W(2d-1).                      (8.4)\n\nThe omitted squared-distance terms are nonnegative. Hence the one\nlater original bridge pays the ENTIRE negative center term whenever\n\n    Q>=c g/v.                                        (8.5)\n\nAll other B_t L_t are then nonnegative. This is an evaluated sufficient\ncondition, involving a ratio whose lower bound is supplied next.\n\n## 9. Mechanism II evaluated on an explicit theta interval\n\nFor g>0 the top root equation is\n\n    rho_+^2-[(H_(m-1)-2)g/T]rho_+-(H_(m-1)-1)=0.\n\nIts positive root is strictly larger than (H_(m-1)-2)g/T. The original\ncenter prefix has sigma_(N/2-1)>exp(-16) by (5.1), so\n\n    Q=sigma_(N/2)\n      >exp(-16) theta (H_(m-1)-2)g/T. Original exact-profile minimax\ntarget OPEN. This is a structural equality of suprema, not a quantitative\nZhang--Jiang comparison. No mathematical program or formal call was used.\n\n## 1. Precise proposed theorem\n\nFix any finite n>=0 and any predetermined finite nonnegative word\nh=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)). Normalize advertised smoothness to one. Let W_n(h)\nbe the original supremum of2(f(x_n)-f_star)/||x_0-x_star||^2 over all\nfinite-dimensional convex differentiable1-smooth functions with an attained\nminimum and x_0!=x_star, under x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.\n\nConsider the following subclass in arbitrary finite Euclidean dimensions D.\nChoose a compact polytope K with at most n+2 vertices, containing0, and a\nvector lambda such that\n\n    <lambda,Y> >0 for every nonzero Y in K.\n\nThus0 is the exposed unique minimizer of the linear functional on K\n(K={0} is allowed). Define the ONE globally specified function\n\n    Phi_(K,lambda)(X)\n       = min_(Y in K) [<lambda,Y>+||X-Y||^2/2].       (1.1)\n\nRestrict the nonzero initial point further by <lambda,X_0>=0. Run the\nsame original word h on this function in the usual Euclidean metric.\nLet W_n^aff(h) be the supremum of2Phi(X_n)/||X_0||^2 over these choices.\n\nThe candidate theorem is\n\n    W_n^aff(h)=W_n(h) for EVERY finite n and h>=0.     (1.2)\n\nEvery member of the restricted class is globally convex differentiable\n1-smooth with UNIQUE minimizer0. A strictly smooth strongly convex source\ntrajectory is represented with exact horizontal dynamics in just one extra\ncoordinate; its terminal objective increases by an explicit nonnegative\nO(B^-2) term. General source functions are then covered by regularization\nand finite-horizon continuity. No uniform approximation in n,h is claimed\nor needed for the fixed-word equality of suprema.\n\nThe restriction in(1.1) concerns the Moreau envelope of a linear functional\nplus a polytope indicator. Its gradient is X-P_K(X-lambda), NOT P_K(X).\nIt must not be confused with the previously studied projection-gradient\npotential or its polar-cone minimizers.\n\n## 2. Direct properties of the target class\n\nCompleting the square gives\n\n    Y(X)=P_K(X-lambda),\n    Phi(X)=<lambda,X>-||lambda||^2/2\n                                  +dist(X-lambda,K)^2/2. (2.1)\n\nProjection onto the nonempty compact convex K exists and is unique. Its\nnormal condition is\n\n    <X-lambda-Y(X),Z-Y(X)> <=0 for every Z in K.      (2.2)\n\nFor two inputs U,V, adding their normal inequalities gives\n<P_K(U)-P_K(V),U-V>>=||P_K(U)-P_K(V)||^2. Consequently I-P_K is\n1-Lipschitz, since expanding its squared difference gives at most||U-V||^2.\nThe usual two trial-point comparisons for squared distance, followed by\nthis continuity, prove its differentiability with gradient I-P_K.\nThus(2.1) is differentiable and\n\n    grad Phi(X)=X-P_K(X-lambda),                     (2.3)\n\nwith Lipschitz constant at most one. Convexity also follows directly from\n(1.1): for minimizers Y_U,Y_V the convex combination lies in K, the linear\nterm averages exactly, and convexity of squared norm bounds Phi at the\ncorresponding convex combination of inputs.\n\nBoth terms in(1.1) are nonnegative. Their sum is zero iff Y=0 and X=Y,\nusing the strict exposing property. Hence Phi>=0 and its complete minimum\nset is exactly{0}. These facts prove W_n^aff(h)<=W_n(h), including the\nspecified normalization and the additional initial orthogonality restriction.\n\n## 3. Strict source and its exact interpolation slack\n\nFirst let f:R^d->R be mu-strongly convex and ell-smooth, where mu>0 and\n0<ell<1. Translate and subtract a constant so its unique minimizer is0\nand f(0)=0. Fix any x_0!=0 and its ACTUAL finite original trajectory.\nFor EVERY i=0,...,n, including the actual unqueried terminal, write\n\n    g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i),\n    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nAlso use the optimum labels x_*=g_*=y_*=0,f_*=delta_*=0. Set\ngamma=(1/ell-1)/2>0. Every ordered original row satisfies\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>\n        <=-gamma||g_i-g_j||^2.                     (3.1)\n\nFor completeness, the stronger coefficient1/(2ell) follows by applying\nthe elementary smooth descent inequality to q(x)=f(x)-<g_i,x>.\nThis convex ell-smooth function is minimized at x_i, so\nq(x_i)<=q(x_j-(1/ell)grad q(x_j))\n       <=q(x_j)-||g_j-g_i||^2/(2ell).\nRearranging proves(3.1). The smooth descent inequality follows by\nintegrating the Lipschitz gradient along that one segment; no external\ninterpolation theorem is needed beyond this argument.\n\nIn particular Q_(*,i)=-delta_i gives\n\n    delta_i>=gamma||g_i||^2>=0.                    (3.2)\n\nStrong convexity makes the gradient injective: its strong monotonicity\nimplies g_i=g_j only if x_i=x_j, and then y_i=y_j and delta_i=delta_j.\nAlso g_i=0 iff x_i=0, in which case y_i=delta_i=0. These degenerate\ncases are essential when the word has zero steps or revisits a point.\n\n## 4. One extra coordinate with fixed affine heights\n\nDefine scalar numbers entirely from the fixed finite source trajectory:\n\n    z_0=0,\n    z_(i+1)=(1-h_i)z_i+h_i delta_i, 0<=i<n,\n    e_i=z_i-delta_i, 0<=i<=n.                       (4.1)\n\nThese are symbolic finite definitions, not an executed numerical\nrecurrence. They do not depend on the forthcoming lift magnitude B.\n\nFor B>0 put in R^(d+1)\n\n    lambda_B=(0,B),\n    Y_i=(y_i,delta_i/B),\n    X_i=(x_i,z_i/B),\n    G_i=X_i-Y_i=(g_i,e_i/B),\n    K_B=conv{0,Y_0,...,Y_n}.                       (4.2)\n\nK_B has at most n+2 vertices. Every nonzero Y_i has delta_i>0 by\n(3.2) and injectivity; if delta_i=0 then Y_i=0. Therefore the linear\nfunctional lambda_B exposes0 strictly on the WHOLE K_B: a convex\ncombination with zero height can use only zero vertices. This proves\nthe required minimum property rather than merely assuming it.\n\nWe now choose a finite B that makes every Y_i the actual projection\nP_(K_B)(X_i-lambda_B). For each i in{0,...,n} and j in{*,0,...,n},\ndirect expansion gives\n\n    <G_i-lambda_B,Y_j-Y_i>\n       =Q_ij+e_i(delta_j-delta_i)/B^2.              (4.3)\n\nWhen g_i=g_j, the two points and heights agree, so BOTH sides of the\nnormal test are exactly zero. Otherwise(3.1) has a strictly negative\nmargin. Define the finite nonnegative scalar\n\n    M=max { [e_i(delta_j-delta_i)]_+\n                       /(gamma||g_i-g_j||^2): g_i!=g_j }, (4.4)\n\nwhere an empty maximum is0. Choosing B^2>=max(1,2M) makes(4.3)\nat most -gamma||g_i-g_j||^2/2 for every distinct-gradient pair.\nAll other pairs are the exact zero cases already handled. Because K_B\nis the convex hull of precisely these points, this checks EVERY normal\ninequality, not just adjacent or active rows. At the optimum, projection\nP_(K_B)(-lambda_B)=0 also follows directly from its exposing property.\n\nThus(2.2)--(2.3) prove simultaneously, for the ONE static Phi_B of(1.1),\n\n    P_(K_B)(X_i-lambda_B)=Y_i,\n    grad Phi_B(X_i)=G_i,\n    Phi_B(X_i)=delta_i+||G_i||^2/2\n               =f_i+e_i^2/(2B^2), 0<=i<=n.         (4.5)\n\nIncluding terminal n in the construction is allowed: an adversarial\nfunction may depend on the whole predetermined word, and is fixed once\nconstructed. No sample is reselected during this actual lifted run.\n\n## 5. Actual original dynamics and exact initial normalization\n\nFor EVERY original update, (4.1)--(4.2) give exactly\n\n    X_i-h_i G_i\n       =(x_i-h_i g_i,[(1-h_i)z_i+h_i delta_i]/B)\n       =X_(i+1).                                  (5.1)\n\nThus the lifted points are actual GD iterates of the one function Phi_B,\nwith the SAME steps. The horizontal coordinates agree with the source\nexactly, not approximately; all vertical motion is paid and retained.\n\nThe initial point is X_0=(x_0,0), so\n\n    <lambda_B,X_0>=0,\n    dist(X_0,argmin Phi_B)^2=||x_0||^2.             (5.2)\n\nThis is the TRUE closest-minimum distance because the minimum is unique0.\nAt the actual terminal,\n\n    2Phi_B(X_n)/||X_0||^2\n       =2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2+e_n^2/(B^2||x_0||^2)\n       >=2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2.                       (5.3)\n\nEvery term is finite. Choosing larger B reduces the additional objective,\nbut no limit in B is needed for the domination inequality. A large lift\nvector is allowed; its norm is not part of the initial radius. The equality\nof initial distances has been proved, not inferred by dropping a coordinate.\n\n## 6. General convex smooth source by finite-horizon regularization\n\nNow take ANY original1-smooth convex differentiable f with a selected\nminimizer x_star, and any x_0!=x_star. Translate that selected minimizer\nto0 and subtract f_star. Other minima are allowed at this stage. For\n0<epsilon<1/2 define\n\n    f_epsilon(x)=(1-2epsilon)f(x)+(epsilon/2)||x||^2. (6.1)\n\nIt has unique minimizer0, is epsilon-strongly convex, and is\nell_epsilon=1-epsilon smooth. The strict-source construction therefore\napplies to its actual GD run from the SAME x_0 for the SAME fixed word.\n\nFor fixed n and h, these source runs converge to the original one as\nepsilon decreases to0. A direct finite induction proves this: if\nx_i^epsilon->x_i, continuity and Lipschitzness of grad f imply\ngrad f_epsilon(x_i^epsilon)->grad f(x_i), and the update with the fixed\nfinite number h_i then gives x_(i+1)^epsilon->x_(i+1). Function-value\ncontinuity and the explicit small quadratic in(6.1) yield\n\n    f_epsilon(x_n^epsilon)->f(x_n).                (6.2)\n\nFor EACH epsilon, (5.3) constructs a member of the restricted class\nwith at most n+2 vertices and orthogonal initial point whose payoff is\nat least2f_epsilon(x_n^epsilon)/||x_0||^2. Taking the limit in these\nscalar inequalities gives\n\n    W_n^aff(h)>=2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2.\n\nThe source f, selected minimizer and initial point were arbitrary, so\nW_n^aff(h)>=W_n(h). Together with Section2 this proves the proposed\nequality(1.2) within this JOINT UNVERIFIED draft.\n\nFor arbitrary advertised L>0 first replace f by f/L after translating\nthe chosen minimizer; the normalized updates are exactly h_i grad(f/L)\nand the payoff remains W=2R. Thus the reduction respects the original\nnormalization. Zero steps, repeated points, hitting the minimum, empty\nwords, arbitrary finite dimensions and arbitrarily large finite steps\nwere not excluded. No infinite-dimensional limit or uniform error claim\nacross words/horizons is used.\n\n## 7. Allocation complete,zero mathematical/formal calls;original target OPEN.", "evidence": "# Explicit Gray initializer: a center-row failure and complete scalar-blend exclusion\n\n**JOINT UNVERIFIED.** Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`.\nOne bounded direct research invocation, allocated model `gpt-6-astra`,\neffort `ultra`, and exactly TWO substantive analytical mechanisms.\nNo numerical or mathematical program was run. No verifier acceptance is\nclaimed. The original all-word exact-profile minimax target is OPEN.\n\nThis report treats every integer m>=1536 with precisely the requested\npre-run geometry\n\n    R=sqrt(3), v=R^(-2)=1/3, zeta=1,\n    delta_0=delta_1=4, theta=0.\n\nAll N original nonzero vertices, the original zero vertex, every tiny\nmixed character, every physical ruler-word step, and the true terminal\nindex n=N-1 are retained.\n\nThe requested lower-decrement initialization can be reconstructed\nexactly, but it is NOT legal. Mechanism I displays its strictly positive\noriginal query-(N/2-1) normal against vertex v_(N/2+1). Mechanism II then\nexcludes the ENTIRE adjacent-blend family lambda in [0,1] by exact\nincompatible original-row intervals. In fact the second argument rules\nout every horizontal initializer in the REQUIRED initial slice\nz_0=(x_0,-sqrt(3)) having this prescribed radial itinerary.\nNo payoff, actual prescribed terminal, or Huber/ray improvement is\nclaimed from an invalid initialization.\n\nThe second exclusion evaluates the complete terminal budget at v=1/3.\nThis is outside the v>=M/2 band established in the preceding public\nbinary-budget draft. Every needed premise is derived below; none of\nthose earlier unverified exclusions is imported as an accepted fact.\n\n## 1. Word, dictionary, roots, and all parameter guards\n\nKeep the enclosing m fixed, including inside every child block, and set\n\n    N=2^m, n=N-1, eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,\n    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    H_l=1+a c^l, h_i=H_(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n.\n\nAll steps are positive and are chosen before the function. Since\neps<1/1000, 1+sqrt(2)<17/7, and\n(17/7)(1001/1000)<49/20, we have\n\n    beta>999/1000, 2<c<49/20<5/2, 2/5<a<1/2.\n\nHere a>(999/1000)/(49/20)=999/2450>2/5. Thus H_0=1+a<2,\nH_1=1+beta<2, and H_l>2 for l>=2. The very weak bounds N>=1024\nand Kmix:=N-1-m>=N/2>=m suffice for the finite-size estimates below; both\nfollow immediately for m>=1536 (the corresponding inequalities persist\nunder doubling N when m increases).\n\nFor every nonzero character t in F_2^m put tau_k=N-2^k and\n\n    eta=1/(N^2 Kmix),\n    b_(tau_k)^2=2^k/(N-1), 0<=k<m,\n    b_t^2=eta for every remaining nonzero t,\n    U_s(t)=b_t chi_t(s), chi_t(s)=(-1)^(t dot s),\n    M=sum_(t!=0)b_t^2=1+1/N^2,\n    T=M+1/3=4/3+1/N^2,\n    w_s=(U_s,1/sqrt(3)), Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2).\n\nEvery b_t is strictly positive. In particular eta>0, eta<=1/N^2,\neta*m<=1/N^2, and 4/3<T<3/2. Write w_i=w_(Gamma(i)) only\nfor original time indices i=0,...,n.\n\nGray bit l is bit_l(i) xor bit_(l+1)(i), with bit_m(i)=0.\nXoring from k through m-1 telescopes, giving\n\n    chi_(tau_k)(Gamma(i))=(-1)^bit_k(i).               (1.1)\n\nIncrementing the original index flips Gray bit nu_2(i+1). Exactly l+1\ndominant characters have bit l set. The full flip energy and Gram are\ntherefore\n\n    E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta(N/2-l-1),\n    G_l=T-2E_l\n       =1/3+(N+1-2^(l+2))/(N-1)+eta(2l+1-m).          (1.2)\n\nAll these are full Euclidean Gram values, including the vertical\ncoordinate. Let t_l=G_l/T and define the positive root\n\n    P_l(r)=r^2+(H_l-2)t_l r+1-H_l,\n    rho_l=[-(H_l-2)t_l\n       +sqrt((H_l-2)^2 t_l^2+4(H_l-1))]/2.\n\nThe constant term is negative, so this polynomial has one positive and\none negative root. Moreover P_l(1)=(2-H_l)(1-t_l). Since E_l>0,\nwe have 0<rho_l<1 at l=0,1 and rho_l>1 at l>=2. The requested law is\n\n    x:=r_0=rho_0(1-4/N), y:=r_1=rho_1(1-4/N),\n    r_l=1 for l>=2,\n    sigma_0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i r_(nu_2(i+1)),\n    v_i=sigma_i w_i.\n\nIn particular 0<x,y<1 and 0<sigma_i<=1 for every original i,\nincluding n. No high-level root is substituted for the prescribed\nhigh-level ratio 1.\n\nFor a short step H_l=1+d the root equation rearranges to\n\n    (1-rho_l)(rho_l+d)=2(1-d)E_l rho_l/T.             (1.3)\n\nDirectly from (1.2), E_0<2/N. For example\n1/(N-1)<=1/N+2/N^2 and eta(N/2-1)<=1/N^2 give this\nstrict bound for the present N. Equation (1.3) implies\n\n    1-rho_0<4/N, 1-x<8/N, x>1/2.                    (1.4)\n\nThe adjacent firm gap at a step of level l is exactly\n\n    k_i:=U_i-L_i=-sigma_i^2 T P_l(r_l)>0,             (1.5)\n\nwhere L_i,U_i are defined in Section 3. For low levels, r_l is\nstrictly between 0 and rho_l, so P_l(r_l)<0. For high levels,\n\n    k_i=2sigma_i^2(H_l-2)E_l>0.\n\nThus all adjacent firm inequalities hold strictly, without implying\nany remote or zero-vertex normal.\n\n## 2. Full Gram, binary radii, static function, and complete minimum\n\nPairing binary labels differing in a bit proves Walsh orthogonality.\nThe Gray map is an invertible linear map over F_2. Its remaining\ncharacters, after the m characters in (1.1), are precisely the binary\ncharacters other than the singleton characters. Summing this complement\nexactly gives, for all original i,j,\n\n    <w_i,w_j>=1/3-eta\n       +sum_(k<m)[2^k/(N-1)-eta](-1)^(bit_k(i)+bit_k(j))\n       +eta N 1_(i=j).                               (2.1)\n\nThe diagonal is exactly T. The last term and every subtraction of eta\nare retained. Nothing here treats mixed characters as zero.\n\nThe number of level-l transitions before query i is\n\n    floor(i/2^l)-floor(i/2^(l+1))\n       =floor(i/2^(l+1))+bit_l(i).\n\nExpanding each floor in the higher bits therefore proves the exact\nbinary product identities\n\n    Q_k=r_k product_(l<k)r_l^(2^(k-l-1)),\n    sigma_i=product_(k<m)Q_k^bit_k(i),\n    Q_0=x, Q_1=xy, Q_k=p^(2^(k-2)) for k>=2,\n    p=x^2 y in (0,1),\n    sigma_(4q+b)=p^q s_b,\n    (s_0,s_1,s_2,s_3)=(1,x,xy,p).                    (2.2)\n\nThese are identities from level counts, not a numerical trajectory\nreplay. In particular every Q_k is in (0,1). Distributivity gives\n\n    sum_(i<N) sigma_i^b (-1)^(xi dot bits(i))\n       =product_(k<m)[1+(-1)^xi_k Q_k^b]              (2.3)\n\nfor any integer b and any binary character xi, including b=-1.\n\nThe fixed body and function are\n\n    D=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n} in R^N,\n    F_D(z)=max_(g in D)[<z,g>-||g||^2/2].\n\nThe full Walsh matrix, including its constant row, is invertible.\nHence the N frames, and then their positive radial rescalings, are\nlinearly independent. Thus D is a full-dimensional simplex.\nCompleting the square identifies the unique maximizer with P_D(z),\nthe Euclidean projection. Its exact necessary and sufficient condition is\n\n    g in D and <z-g,w-g><=0 for EVERY vertex w of D.   (2.4)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiation along each segment; sufficiency\nfollows by expanding squared distance and then taking convex\ncombinations. Adding two such normal conditions proves firm\nnonexpansiveness of P_D and hence its 1-Lipschitz property. Comparison\nof the two maximizing affine expressions bounds the differentiability\nremainder by ||d|| ||P_D(z+d)-P_D(z)||<=||d||^2.\nTherefore F_D is finite, convex, differentiable, and\ngrad F_D=P_D is 1-Lipschitz. On the interior of D it is ||z||^2/2,\nso the least smoothness constant is exactly 1.\n\nLet C=cone{v_i}. This cone is closed as an invertible linear image\nof the nonnegative orthant. Its complete polar is the minimum set:\n\n    min F_D=0,\n    argmin F_D=C^polar\n       ={(x',y'): <x',U_s>+y'/sqrt(3)<=0 for every s}. (2.5)\n\nA polar point has value zero. A positive dot product with any generator\ngives positive value on a sufficiently short segment toward that\ngenerator, proving the converse. Positive radii do not change the cone\nor this minimum set. Multiplying F_D by L>0 supplies the original\nL-smooth normalization and the same physical GD word.\n\n## 3. Exact reconstruction and formulas for EVERY original row\n\nDefine\n\n    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t v_t,\n    A_i=||v_i||^2/2+<P_i,v_i>,\n    L_t=h_t<v_t,v_(t+1)>+||v_t-v_(t+1)||^2/2,\n    U_t=h_t||v_t||^2-||v_t-v_(t+1)||^2/2,\n    H=sum_(i=0)^n 1/sigma_i=product_(k<m)(1+Q_k^(-1)).\n\nFor mechanism I take Delta_t=L_t. Set exactly as requested\n\n    C_i=sum_(t<i)Delta_t,\n    f_0=[-N-sum_i(A_i-C_i)/sigma_i]/H,\n    f_i=f_0-C_i, F_i=f_i+A_i, q_i=F_i/sigma_i,\n    x_(0,t)=(1/(N b_t))sum_i chi_t(Gamma(i))q_i,\n    z_0=(x_0,-sqrt(3)), z_i=z_0-P_i.                 (3.1)\n\nEvery sum includes all original indices specified by its bounds;\nin particular i runs through n in H and the slice relation.\nThe definition of f_0 makes sum_i q_i=-N. The nonconstant Walsh\nkernel is N 1_(i=j)-1, so direct substitution yields\n\n    <x_0,U_(Gamma(j))>=q_j+1,\n    <z_0,w_j>=q_j, <z_0,v_j>=F_j,\n    <z_j,v_j>-||v_j||^2/2=f_j.                       (3.2)\n\nThus these formulas reconstruct the prescribed formal values exactly.\nThey are ACTUAL F_D values and gradients only if every condition\n(2.4) holds. The complete, unreduced original normal array is\n\n    R_ij:=<z_i-v_i,v_j-v_i>\n       =C_i-C_j+<v_j,P_j-P_i>+||v_j-v_i||^2/2<=0,\n       0<=i,j<=n,                                    (3.3)\n\nand EVERY original zero-vertex row is\n\n    R_i,zero:=<z_i-v_i,-v_i>\n       =T sigma_i^2/2-f_i<=0, 0<=i<=n.              (3.4)\n\nThere are no omitted nonzero or zero-vertex inequalities. Formulas\n(3.3)-(3.4) specify all of them; the exact violated rows below disprove\ntheir simultaneous satisfaction. They do not assert that unchecked\nremote rows follow from adjacent ones.\n\nThe adjacent specializations are\n\n    R_(t,t+1)=L_t-Delta_t,\n    R_(t+1,t)=Delta_t-U_t.                            (3.5)\n\nFor the lower law the former equals zero and the latter equals -k_t<0.\nFor these prescribed displacement points the vertical coordinate is\nexactly\n\n    (z_i)_vertical=-sqrt(3)-(1/sqrt(3))sum_(t<i)h_t sigma_t. (3.6)\n\nThis is also the actual vertical drift IF the prescribed vertices are\nthe actual gradients. The initial vertical slice is never falsely\nheld fixed after time zero.\n\n## 4. Mechanism I: an explicitly positive ORIGINAL center normal\n\nTake the original indices\n\n    q=N/2-1, q+1=N/2, q+2=N/2+1,\n    J=H_(m-1), s=sigma_q=p^(N/8)>0,\n    p_0=v_q=s w_q,\n    p_1=v_(q+1)=s w_(q+1),\n    p_2=v_(q+2)=s x w_(q+2).\n\nThe first physical step is the unique top step J; its radius ratio is\nexactly 1. The next physical step is H_0, with radius ratio x.\nAll three queries are original and lie before the true terminal.\n\nPut\n\n    g=-G_(m-1)=2/3-eta(m-1)>0,\n    d=2/(N-1)-2eta>1/N.\n\nThe positivity follows from eta*m<=1/N^2. Formula (2.1) gives exactly\n\n    <w_q,w_(q+1)>=-g,\n    <w_(q+1),w_(q+2)>=T-2E_0,\n    <w_q,w_(q+2)>=-g+d.                              (4.1)\n\nIndeed the last endpoint differs from the middle endpoint only in\nbinary bit 0, changing the first singleton summand from minus to plus.\nThe mixed-character subtraction -2eta in d is included.\n\nExpanding the two lower decrements in (3.3) gives the exact identity\n\n    R_(q,q+2)\n       =-L_q-L_(q+1)+J<p_0,p_2>\n          +H_0<p_1,p_2>+||p_2-p_0||^2/2\n       =<(J-1)p_0+p_1,p_2-p_1>\n       =s^2[(J-1)((1-x)g+x d)-(1-x)T-2x E_0].       (4.2)\n\nThis is an original query-q projection normal against the original\nvertex v_(q+2). It does not depend on a favorable f_0 or terminal\nbudget, and it does not use any deleted intermediate sample.\n\nBy (1.4), x>1/2, 1-x<8/N, E_0<2/N, and T<3/2. The g term\nis nonnegative. Consequently\n\n    R_(q,q+2)>s^2[(J-1)/(2N)-16/N]\n              =s^2(J-33)/(2N)>0.                    (4.3)\n\nThe final inequality is uniform: J-1=a c^(m-1)>(2/5)2^(m-1)>64\nfor all m>=1536. Thus the exact initializer (3.1) is decisively\nillegal, despite its exact Walsh reconstruction and strictly feasible\nadjacent firm gaps. This concludes mechanism I before beginning the\nsecond mechanism.\n\n## 5. Mechanism II: the ENTIRE adjacent-blend family\n\nKeep every word, vertex, and parameter fixed. For any lambda in [0,1],\nreplace only\n\n    Delta_t(lambda)=L_t+lambda k_t,\n    C_i(lambda)=sum_(t<i)Delta_t(lambda)\n\nand reconstruct f_i,F_i,q_i,x_0,z_0 by (3.1). All those quantities\nare affine in lambda. Every original normal in (3.3)-(3.4) is also\naffine in lambda. By (3.5), the original adjacent rows are exactly\n\n    R_(t,t+1)=-lambda k_t,\n    R_(t+1,t)=-(1-lambda)k_t.                         (5.1)\n\nSince k_t>0, these original rows require 0<=lambda<=1. We will now\nderive an exact incompatible interval from the ORIGINAL terminal\nzero-vertex row, rather than leave an unevaluated intersection.\n\nDefine the positive weights and full budget\n\n    B_t=sum_(i=0)^t 1/sigma_i,\n    Z=sum_(i=0)^n A_i/sigma_i+sum_(t<n)B_t L_t,\n    K=sum_(t<n)B_t k_t>0.\n\nThe initial slice relation is\nsum_i(f_i+A_i)/sigma_i=-N. Substituting\nf_i=f_n+sum_(t=i)^(n-1)Delta_t and exchanging finite sums gives\n\n    f_n(lambda)=(-N-Z-lambda K)/H.                    (5.2)\n\nThe two parts of Z are evaluated completely enough to prove its sign\nbelow. No favorable but unevaluated terminal budget is called a witness.\n\n## 6. Exact suffix-Gram sums and the first part of Z\n\nFor an original step index t let\n\n    Ttail_t=sum_(i>t)<w_t,w_i>.\n\nPartition this actual suffix by the highest binary bit k at which i\nand t differ. Such a block occurs exactly when bit_k(t)=0, contains\n2^k elements, agrees in the higher bits, has opposite bit k, and has\nuniform lower bits. Averaging (2.1) on it gives\n\n    V_k=1/3+(N-3*2^k)/(N-1)-eta(m-k-1),\n    Ttail_t=sum_(k:bit_k(t)=0)2^k V_k.                (6.1)\n\nAll suffix indices remain chronological. The diagonal term is absent\nbecause every index in the suffix differs from t. Let\n\n    X_l=sum_(t:nu_2(t+1)=l)sigma_t Ttail_t.\n\nAt such t, lower bits are 1, bit l is 0, and higher bits are free.\nUsing (2.2) and summing each free bit exactly yields\n\n    X_l=(product_(j<l)Q_j)(product_(j>l)(1+Q_j))\n       *[2^l V_l+sum_(k>l)2^k V_k/(1+Q_k)].         (6.2)\n\nWe prove that all X_l other than the top are positive, and that two\ncomplete generations pay the negative top contribution with margin.\nThe exact top three V values are\n\n    V_(m-1)=-1/6-1/[2(N-1)]<0,\n    V_(m-2)=7/12+1/[4(N-1)]-eta,\n    V_(m-3)=23/24+5/[8(N-1)]-2eta.                   (6.3)\n\nEvery V_k with k<=m-2 is positive, since\nV_k>=7/12-eta*m>0.\n\nAt l=m-2 the bracket in (6.2) is at least\nN[V_(m-2)/4+V_(m-1)/2]>0, as quantified below.\nFor every l<=m-3, its bracket is strictly larger than\n\n    N[V_(m-2)/8+V_(m-3)/16+V_(m-1)/2]\n      =N[19/384-23/[128(N-1)]-eta/4]>0.              (6.4)\n\nFor positive terms this uses 1/(1+Q_k)>1/2; an own-level term is\neven larger. For the negative top term its denominator is safely\nreplaced by 1. Every remaining term is nonnegative. The final\nsubtracted correction in (6.4) is less than 1/(2N): use\nN-1>N/2, eta<=1/N^2, and N>=1024. This proves X_l>0 for\nall l<m-1, including both damped low levels.\n\nAt the top bridge q=N/2-1, (6.1) gives\n\n    Ttail_q=-N/12-N/[4(N-1)]>-253N/3000.              (6.5)\n\nHere 253/3000=1/12+1/1000, and\n1/[4(N-1)]<1/1000. The two original quarter bridges obey\n\n    Ttail_(N/4-1)/N\n       =1/16-3/[16(N-1)]-eta/4>1/18,\n    Ttail_(3N/4-1)/N\n       =7/48+1/[16(N-1)]-eta/4>1/7.                 (6.6)\n\nThe correction in the first line is less than 1/(2N)<1/144;\nthe second follows from 7/48-1/7=1/336 and eta/4<1/336.\nAmong the four original eighth bridges, the smallest suffix value is\nat 3N/8-1, and it obeys\n\n    Ttail_(3N/8-1)/N\n       =7/192-11/[64(N-1)]-eta/4>1/32.              (6.7)\n\nIts correction is less than 1/(2N)<1/192. The other three are\nlarger by adding the positive quarter contribution and/or removing\nthe negative top contribution. Thus ALL four eighth values exceed\nN/32. These estimates retain the finite-N and eta terms exactly\nbefore bounding them in their adverse directions.\n\nThe bridge radii are actual binary factors, not restarted child radii.\nLet u=p^(N/16), w=p^(N/32), so s=p^(N/8)=u^2=w^4.\nThe quarter radii are u,u^3 and the eighth radii are w,w^3,w^5,w^7.\nEquations (6.6)-(6.7) and elementary square nonnegativity give\n\n    X_(m-2)>N(u/18+u^3/7)\n       =Ns[1/(18u)+u/7]>Ns/6,\n    X_(m-3)>(N/32)(w+w^3+w^5+w^7)>=Ns/8.            (6.8)\n\nFor the first bound, the bracket is at least 2/sqrt(126)>1/6,\nsince 144>126. For the second, pair w^(-3) with w^3 and\nw^(-1) with w, each pair having sum at least 2.\n\nH_(m-2)>J/c>(2/5)J and H_(m-3)>J/c^2>(4/25)J.\nDiscarding only the now-proved positive remaining X_l, (6.5)-(6.8)\ngive the evaluated FULL cross contribution\n\n    sum_(t<n)h_t sigma_t Ttail_t=sum_l H_l X_l\n       >NJs[-253/3000+(2/5)(1/6)+(4/25)(1/8)]\n       =(7/3000)NJs>0.                              (6.9)\n\nFinally, expanding A_i and exchanging the finite sums proves exactly\n\n    sum_i A_i/sigma_i\n       =(T/2)sum_i sigma_i+sum_(t<n)h_t sigma_t Ttail_t\n       >(T/2)sum_i sigma_i+(7/3000)NJs.              (6.10)\n\nNo original step, terminal term, or potentially negative summand was\ndiscarded without its sign being established or its price being paid.\n\n## 7. Four bridge generations evaluate the other part of Z\n\nDefine W(t)=B_t sigma_t^2 and\nW_l=sum_(t:nu_2(t+1)=l)W(t). For every high-level bridge,\nt=4q+3. The four-query inverse-radius mass is\n\n    1+1/x+1/(xy)+1/p=(1+x+xy+p)/p.\n\nSumming the actual geometric prefix, with d0=1+x+xy+p, gives\n\n    B_t sigma_t=[d0/(1-p)](1-sigma_t),\n    W(t)=[d0/(1-p)]sigma_t(1-sigma_t).               (7.1)\n\nThis keeps the entire prefix B_t, including translated child blocks.\nTo compare two child bridges with their parent, first note that for\n0<z<1,\n\n    [z(1-z)+z^3(1-z^3)]/[z^2(1-z^2)]\n       =[z^(-1)+z+z^2+z^3]/(1+z)>3/2. (7.3)\n\nThe last bound uses sigma_i<=1 at every query in the true prefix.\nAll four selected levels are >=2, so their ratios are exactly 1.\n\nFor a general level l, the exact lower decrement is\n\n    L_t=sigma_t^2 ell_l,\n    ell_l=H_l r_l G_l+(T/2)(1-r_l)^2+2r_l E_l. (7.6)\n\nThus both complete pieces of the original terminal budget are paid,\nand their evaluated combination is\n\n    Z>(T/2)sum_i sigma_i+(NJ/3000)(7s+101s^2)>0. No claim is made for arbitrary initial vertical coordinates.\nThis conclusion is about the prescribed itinerary and fixed geometry;\nit is not an upper bound on the worst-case value over all functions.\n\n## 9. For either\nreconstruction define\n\n    Mscore=max_(0<=i<=n) F_i/sigma_i=max_i q_i,\n    z_star=(x_0,-sqrt(3)-sqrt(3)Mscore).\n\nEquations (3.2) and (2.5) give directly\n\n    <z_star,w_i>=q_i-Mscore<=0,\n    z_star in C^polar=argmin F_D,\n    ||z_0-z_star||=sqrt(3)|Mscore|. This allocation is not renewed.\n\nThe audited mathematical assertion concerns the specific same-enclosing-parameter word `H_m`, for every integer `m >= 1536`, on finite scalar plateau envelopes and their finite-dimensional orthogonal exposed-segment realizations. It is not an upper bound over arbitrary polytopes or all smooth convex functions.\n\n## 2. Exact conclusion that survived\n\nKeep, for one fixed enclosing integer `m >= 1536`,\n\n```\nepsilon = 1/(4m), beta = 1-epsilon,\nc = (1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a = beta/c, b = a*beta,\nd_j = a*c^j,\nH_0 = (), H_(j+1) = (H_j, 1+d_j, H_j),\nS_j = sum H_j, S = S_m, D = 1+2S, n = 2^m-1.\n```\n\nEvery child uses these SAME `m, epsilon, beta, c, a, b`. The auxiliary `d_m` is a radius, not an added physical step.\n\nFor every finite `L >= 0`, let\n\n```\nphi_L(x) = min_(0<=y<=L) [y + (x-y)^2/2],\ng_L(x) = x - clip(x-1,0,L),\nT_h(x) = x-h*g_L(x),\nPsi_j = chronological composition along the complete H_j,\nq_L(s) = 2*phi_L(Psi_m(s))/s^2, s != 0.\n```\n\nThe surviving conclusions are exactly:\n\n1. `q_L(s) <= 1/D` for every finite `L >= 0` and every real nonzero `s`, uniformly allowing both to depend arbitrarily on the enclosing `m`.\n2. `sup_(L>=0,s!=0) q_L(s) = 1/D`; a scalar maximizer is `L=2S, s=D`.\n3. For `0 < |s| <= d_m`, `q_L(s) < 1/d_m^2 < 1/D`.\n4. The supremum over all admissible finite orthogonal exposed-segment instances in arbitrary finite dimensions is `1/D`. Each individual admissible nonzero finite instance has value strictly smaller than `1/D`.\n\nThe second assertion is a supremum over BOTH `L` and `s`. It is not an equality for every fixed `L`. In particular, `L=0` gives the quadratic score `Q=p_m^2 < 1/D` for every nonzero start. The distinction between scalar attainment and unattained orthogonal-segment supremum is essential and is correctly preserved in the candidate.\n\nNo first invalid inference was identified. No mathematical repair or counterexample is required by the checks below. This finding records the limits of one analytical audit; it does not replace the project verifier.\n\n## 3. Static objective, exact interval map, and tube\n\n### 3.1 One globally legal function\n\nThe minimizing scalar point is `clip(x-1,0,L)`. Thus the candidate's three branches are\n\n```\nphi_L(x) = x^2/2                  (x <= 1),\n           x-1/2                 (1 <= x <= 1+L),\n           L+(x-L)^2/2           (x >= 1+L).\n```\n\nBoth values and first derivatives agree at each tie. The derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, with slopes `1,0,1`; the unique minimum is zero at zero. The feasible trial `y=0` proves `0 <= phi_L(x) <= x^2/2`. In particular, positive gradients satisfy `0 < g_L(x) <= x`, and negative inputs use exactly `g_L(x)=x`.\n\nAt `L=0` both switches coincide and the function is globally quadratic. The source correctly does not infer either nontrivial endpoint normal implication from the vacuous projection normal on the singleton. Every normal and sample belongs to this one static function; there is no independent choice of forcing values.\n\n### 3.2 Signed endpoint map\n\nFor `h=1+d`, the complete map has slopes `-d,1,-d` on the three successive branches. The image of an interval containing zero remains an interval containing zero by continuity. For `p,q >= 0`, its exact endpoints are\n\n```\nT_h([-p,q]) = [-p',q'],\np' = max(d*min(q,1), d*q-h*L),\nq' = max(d*p, min(q,1+L)-h).\n```\n\nThe lower endpoint comes from the endpoint of the decreasing core branch or the far upper endpoint. The upper endpoint comes from the negative endpoint or the end of the increasing plateau branch. If `q <= 1`, the unused plateau expression is negative and the unused upper expression cannot exceed `d*q`. If `1 < q < 1+L`, the unused upper expression is at most `d`. Therefore the displayed formula is valid without an implicit branch condition. The cases `q=0,1,1+L` and `L=0` are included.\n\nExactness persists under chronological composition: every continuous real image of an interval is an interval, so the next endpoint update receives the actual image. No unsupported symmetric replacement or filling of a disconnected set occurs.\n\n### 3.3 Product and mass inputs\n\nThe mass identity `A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j`, with `A_j=1+S_j`, has the stated closed solution\n\n```\nA_j = (1-eta)*2^j + eta*c^j, eta=a/(c-2) in (0,1).\n```\n\nIt gives `S_j >= d_j/(c-2)-1`, `S<c^m-1`, and `D<2c^m`. The elementary parameter bounds in the source imply\n\n```\n12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2,\n0<b<a, c*b=beta^2<1.\n```\n\nThe literal word product is\n\n```\np_j = beta^(2^j-1)*c^(-j),\nr_j = d_j*p_j = beta^(2^j)/c,\nQ = p_m^2.\n```\n\nEvery nonempty prefix product is at most `a`: a prefix in the first child inherits the bound; after its bridge, the product is `r_j` times a right-child prefix product of at most one. The base is the leaf product `a`. Since the words are palindromes, the same bound holds for nonempty suffixes. This argument uses physical prefixes and suffixes, with no deletion of leaves.\n\nConsequently `DQ<1` and `d_m^2>D`, with the strict inequalities stated in source (9). All these estimates use the enclosing parameters; no asymptotic statement with hidden `L` or `s` dependence enters.\n\n### 3.4 Distance tube is used within its limits\n\nFor `Delta_L(x)=dist(x,[0,L])`, each of the four sign/branch regions gives\n\n`Delta_L(T_(1+d)(x)) <= d*max(Delta_L(x),1)`.\n\nExpanding this maximum symbolically along a complete child yields\n\n`Delta_L(Psi_j(x)) <= max(p_j*Delta_L(x),a)`.\n\nThe inserted constants are multiplied by nonempty physical suffix products, explaining the `a` term. Hence a symmetric input of radius `R` has image inside `[-v,L+v]`, `v=max(p_j R,a)`.\n\nThe tube alone does not control the linear height inside `[0,L]`. The candidate explicitly respects this limitation. Its `L=2S, s=D` plateau example has zero terminal distance to that interval and positive objective. The final proof uses the signed capture and ray-energy arguments, not an invalid conversion of distance into objective.\n\n## 4. Critical Section 6 guard at radius c*d_j\n\nFix `j >= 1`, put `d=d_j`, `R=c*d`, and assume `L>2d`. The source's no-upper-return claim is local to this input radius and this long-segment regime.\n\nAfter the FIRST physical leaf `ell=1+a`, the exact negative endpoint is at most `a`, since\n\n`aR-ell L = beta*d-ell L < 0`.\n\nIts positive endpoint is at most `max(aR,L-a)`, and both terms are below `L`, because `aR=beta d<L`. Thus the first leaf establishes the needed guard even if the original positive start was in the upper wing.\n\nThe remaining physical pattern alternates bridges and leaves. A negative bridge query follows a leaf, whose preceding positive query was guarded, so its magnitude is at most `a`; the bridge returns it to at most `a*d_k`. A guarded positive bridge query can land negatively with magnitude at most `d_k`, and the next leaf returns it to at most `a*d_k`. Positive-to-positive updates decrease the state. Since every internal bridge has `k<=j-1`, each newly created positive state is at most `aR<L`. These observations close the guard induction; they do not presuppose the complete-child capture being proved later.\n\nFor a positive terminal, either there was a negative-to-positive return, whose last occurrence and subsequent positive decrease bound the terminal by `aR`, or every state stayed positive. In the latter case each queried state exceeds one, otherwise its update would be negative. Every queried gradient is therefore at least one, and the terminal is at most `R-S_j`. Thus\n\n`sup_(|s|<=R) [Psi_j(s)]_+ <= max(aR,R-S_j)`.\n\nThe mass bound yields\n\n```\naR=beta*d<=d,\nR-S_j <= [c-1/(c-2)]*d+1 <= 2d+1.\n```\n\nThe last comparison is equivalent to `(c-2)^2<=1`, valid here. This proves the guarded endpoint estimate `q<=1+2d`. The `j=1` case has no internal bridge and is covered by the first-leaf argument. The proof does not give a short child an independent large-depth assumption.\n\n**Audit result:** the first-leaf reset and the leaf/bridge parity justify the restricted no-upper guard. It is not the previously invalid universal no-reactivation claim.\n\n## 5. Critical Section 7: simultaneous scaled complete-child capture\n\nThe exact invariant is\n\n`Psi_j([-d_j*b^t,d_j*b^t]) subset [-b^(t+1),b^(t+1)]`\n\nfor every `1<=j<=m`, every integer `t>=0`, and every finite `L>=0`.\n\nThe base `j=1` uses `d_1=beta<1`, so the leaf is quadratic on every indicated input and its output radius is exactly bounded by `a*beta*b^t=b^(t+1)`.\n\nInduct on `j`, assuming ALL scales `t` for that child. For the parent at `t=0`, the first child receives radius `c*d_j`. Its tube has `v=max(beta^(2^j),a)<=1`, so its exact signed endpoints satisfy `p<=1`, `q<=L+1`. The actual bridge's negative endpoint is at most `d_j` from both terms of the exact endpoint map. Its positive endpoint has one term `d_j*p<=d_j`; for the other term:\n\n- If `L<=2d_j`, `min(q,1+L)-(1+d_j)<=L-d_j<=d_j`.\n- If `L>2d_j`, Section 6 supplies `q<=1+2d_j`, giving the same bound.\n\nHence the bridge's entire image lies in `[-d_j,d_j]`, and the COMPLETE second child maps it into `[-b,b]`. The switch case `L=2d_j` belongs to the first case, so no boundary is omitted.\n\nFor any `t>=1`, `c*b<1` puts the parent input inside the child input at scale `t-1`. The first complete child therefore returns a state in `[-b^t,b^t]`. Because `b^t<1`, the bridge is exactly quadratic on this entire interval; its output lies in `[-d_j*b^t,d_j*b^t]`. The child hypothesis at scale `t` then controls the WHOLE right child. There is no circular use of the parent assertion and no restriction on upper-wing behavior inside the right child.\n\nFor any `0<R<=d_j`, choose the unique relevant annulus\n\n`d_j*b^(t+1)<R<=d_j*b^t`.\n\nThe invariant gives `|Psi_j(s)|<=b^(t+1)<R/d_j` for every `|s|<=R`. If `R` is a boundary power, choosing it as the upper endpoint preserves the strict lower inequality. Taking `R=|s|` gives the strict relative bound with the ORIGINAL scalar radius. It is not a radius reset after a later entry.\n\n**Audit result:** the simultaneous quantifiers in `(j,t,L)` and the strict small-start conclusion are justified.\n\n## 6. Critical Section 8: every physical suffix after a small crossing\n\nA small crossing is an actual query `0<x_i<=1+L` whose landing is negative. At physical level `k`, direct use of the core or plateau formula gives landing magnitude at most `d_k`, including boundary queries. Each ancestor in whose LEFT child it lies contributes exactly its bridge followed by its entire right child. Ancestors in whose right child it lies contribute no further step at that ancestor. The contributing levels strictly increase. This lists the actual suffix in order; it does not replace it by another schedule.\n\nAt each contributing ancestor, the current magnitude is at most `a<1`, so the bridge is quadratic and sends it to magnitude at most `a*d_l<=d_l`. Its entire right child returns magnitude at most `b`. This proves the cap through the physical terminal. If no ancestor remains after a leaf crossing, that leaf was the last physical step, and its magnitude cap is already `a`.\n\nTherefore every trajectory with at least one small crossing has terminal magnitude at most `a`, regardless of intermediate upper-wing reactivations inside the subsequently captured children. Combining with either `|s|>d_m` or the previously proved small-start case gives a strict payoff bound below `1/D` for this branch.\n\n**Audit result:** all suffix positions are covered, including a last-leaf crossing and a crossing deep inside a right subtree. Critical Sections 9-10: chronological exterior prefix and original cuts\n\n### 7.1 Exhaustive exterior-history reduction\n\nOn a history with no small crossing and no zero hit, every positive-to-negative crossing queries strictly above `1+L`. A negative state always moves to a positive one. Once a positive-to-positive step occurs, its output is below `1+L`: if its input was upper-wing, its output is `L-d(x-L)<L`; otherwise positive decrease preserves the bound. It never continues that formula through an invalid subsequent query. The same finite `L` appears with its negative sign in every term.\n\nThe symbolic solution is\n\n`e_k=P(k)*e_0-L*B(k)`,\n\nwhere `P(k)` is the product of the first `k` physical excesses and `B(k)` is the stated sum of trailing products.
